14.3 Step 2.5 — Choose Your Cultural Tradition (Per Patch 8.01)
Based on your chosen region, your people follow one of these cultural
traditions:
CENTRAL PLAINS
Heartland Nobility — Traditional lords, honor-bound, agricultural
"My family has held these lands for six generations. The soil remembers us."
Plains Riders — Horse-riding herders, follow the herds, mobile
"The grass is our sea. We ride where the grass is green."
Riverbank Settlers — Mixed farming and fishing, pragmatic
"The river gives, the river takes. We learn to live with both."
WESTERN RIVERS
River Lords — Control the waterways, tolls, and trade
"Every boat pays. Every bridge has a price. This is the way of the river."
Marsh Dwellers — Live in the delta, hidden, self-sufficient
"They cannot find what they cannot see. The reeds hide many things."
Boat People — Live on the water, nomadic rivermen
"Our homes float. Our roads flow. We answer to no lord but the current."
EASTERN FORESTS
Hill Fort Dwellers — Settled, defensive, ancient lineages
"Our fathers built these walls before the first king. They still stand."
Deep Forest Folk — Semi-nomadic, hunters, spirit-guided
"The trees speak. The animals watch. We walk softly and listen."
River Forest People — Canoe culture, fishing, trading
"The river through the trees — that is our road, our market, our home."
SOUTHERN MOUNTAINS
High Clan — Mountain fortresses, clan law, ancestral oaths
"The mountain does not forget. Neither do we."
Valley Farmers — Sheltered valleys, peaceful, self-contained

-- 81 of 529 --

"The mountains protect us. We ask only to be left in peace."
Pass Guardians — Control the routes, tolls, wary of outsiders
"The pass is ours. You cross with permission or not at all."
NORTHERN SNOWLANDS
Fjord Dwellers — Coastal, fishing, longship raids
"The sea gives more than the land. We take from both."
Taiga Hunters — Follow the game, hardy, communal
"Winter tests us. We survive together or not at all."
Hold Folk — Fortified settlements, survival-focused
"Our walls keep out the cold and the enemy. Both wish us dead."
This choice affects:
NPC reactions when you introduce yourself
Cultural bonuses to certain actions (see cultural tables)
Starting equipment flavor
Story opportunities that arise during play
Valid Input: One of the cultural tradition names

14.12 Step 9B — Flavor Origin Resource Modifiers (Per Patch 7.1)
If you provided a flavor detail, your holding gains a permanent resource-related
trait:
Flavor Detail Example Game Effect
"My lands flood every spring." +1 FSU per year (fertile soil), but flood
risk
"My forests are full of elk." +1 FSU from hunting, +1 fur per season
"My hold guards an old bridge." +5 SD/month toll income
"The wind never stops here." -1 travel time for ships, +1 to weather rolls
"Our walls are built from black stone." +1 AC to fortifications
"We move at night." +1 to ambush rolls
"The cave has a hidden spring." Unlimited fresh water during siege
"The locals leave food for us." +1 FSU per week from tributes

PART 14.37 — CHARACTER CREATION QUESTION LOCK
CHARACTER CREATION RULES:
The system will ask ONE question at a time
Only the current step's question will be shown
Only valid options for that step will be listed
Do not provide answers for future steps
Do not add extra details beyond what is asked
Each step requires explicit confirmation before proceeding
If you provide multiple answers at once, ONLY THE CURRENT STEP will be
processed. The rest will be ignored, and you will be asked again for the correct
input.

PART 15: SPIRIT NAMING SYSTEM (Pages 69-72)
15.1 Core Principle
Among tribal and nomadic peoples, every person has two names: an everyday name
used in daily life, and a spirit name received in ceremony, used only in sacred
contexts. Each person also has sacred colors that appear in their regalia.

-- 134 of 529 --

15.2 Everyday Names
Simple, practical names used in daily interaction. Drawn from nature, animals,
or simple descriptors.
Examples: River, Stone, Wolf, Birch, Cloud, Fern, Elk, Rain
15.3 Spirit Names
Received during vision quest or coming-of-age ceremony. Used only in ceremony,
never in casual conversation.
SKY NAMES
Sky Woman · Thunder Child · Cloud Dancer · Storm Caller · Rainbow Walker · Dawn
Bringer · Star Gazer · Lightning Hand · Sun Keeper
EARTH NAMES
River Rain · Mountain Mist · Stone Heart · Valley Fern · Prairie Wind · Clay
Potter · Soil Keeper · Root Digger · Field Breather
FOREST NAMES
Cedar Song · Birch Whisper · Fern Glade · Moss Keeper · Oak Stands Firm · Pine
Needle · Willow Bend · Aspen Shiver · Hemlock Deep
ANIMAL NAMES
Wolf Brother · Elk Runner · Raven Speaker · Bear Heart · Fox Quick-Step · Eagle
Soarer · Salmon Leaper · Owl Watcher · Turtle Carrier
CEREMONY NAMES
Sundance Cedar · Pipe Carrier · Drum Keeper · Sweat Lodge Elder · Vision Seeker
· Prayer Winger · Flame Touched · Ghost Dancer · Star Knower
15.4 Color Associations
Each person has sacred colors revealed during their naming ceremony. These
appear in regalia, face paint, and ceremonial objects.
Color Meaning
Red Life, blood, courage
Yellow Dawn, knowledge, the east
White Snow, purity, winter, the north
Black Night, mystery, the west
Blue Sky, truth, the south
Green Forest, growth, healing
Brown Earth, home, foundation
15.5 Name Meaning Table
When a spirit name is received, it signifies something about the person's nature
or destiny.
Name Element Suggests
sky, Thunder, Storm Leader, visionary, one who speaks truth
River, Rain, Water Healer, mediator, one who flows around obstacles
Stone, Mountain, Root Defender, steadfast one, immovable
Wolf, Bear, Elk Warrior, protector, one who fights for the people
Cedar, Birch, Oak Wisdom-keeper, elder, one who remembers
Fox, Raven, Crane Cunning, messenger, one who sees what others miss
Dawn, Sunrise, Morning New beginning, hope, one who brings light
PART 16: NOMAD NAMING SYSTEM — THE THREE NAMES OF THE STEPPE (Pages 72-77 +
Patch 7.10)
16.1 Core Principle — Three Names
Among the steppe nomads, every person carries three names, but only one is
spoken freely.
Name Type When Used Who Knows It
Trader Name Daily life, markets, strangers Everyone
War Name Battle, challenge, earned deeds Acquaintances, allies
Clan Name Kin, trusted friends, ceremonies Family, blood-sworn brothers

-- 135 of 529 --

16.2 Trader Names — What the World Calls You
Trader names are practical, descriptive, and often change with circumstances.
They are what you are called in markets, camps, and by strangers.
Borte — Grey (for hair or horse)
Khubilai — He who gathers
Yesugei — Like nine (born under nine stars)
Hoelun — Cloud woman
Temujin — Iron worker (smith's son)
Boorchu — Leather worker
Jebe — Arrow (swift, straight)
Subotai — One who hunts with eagles
Muqali — Blunt (honest, direct)
Khasar — Wild beast (untamed)
Temuge — Iron rod (unyielding)
Belgutei — One who stands firm
Jochi — Guest (foundling, adopted)
Chagatai — White (pure, bright)
Ogedei — He who rises
Tolui — Mirror (reflects the clan)
Sorghaghtani — Whorled (like a spiral, wise)
Mongke — Eternal
Ariq — Pure, honest
Bayan — Rich (in horses, in children)
16.3 War Names — What You Earn
War names are not given at birth. They are earned through deeds. A young nomad
may ride to war with only a trader name and return with a name that will be
spoken for generations.
War Name Generation Table
First, roll for the deed:
Roll Deed Name Stem
1 First kill in battle "Slayer of [enemy]"
2 Captured a horse herd "Herd-Taker"
3 Survived a blizzard alone "Ice-Walker"
4 Saved a clanmate "Life-Giver"
5 Shot an eagle from the sky "Eagle-Fall"
6 Rode further than any scout "Horizon-Reacher"
Then roll for the style:
Roll Style Example
1 Direct "Borte the Herd-Taker"

-- 136 of 529 --

2 Poetic "Borte Who Walks Through Ice"
3 Feared "Borte Eagle-Fall"
4 Honored "Borte Life-Giver"
5 Descriptive "Borte Far-Rider"
6 Legendary "Borte Unbroken"
War Names Cannot Be:
Used by anyone who hasn't earned them
Claimed falsely (severe dishonor)
Spoken lightly in the presence of the name-holder's clan
16.4 Clan Names — The Blood You Carry
Clan names are ancient, passed down through generations. They are spoken only
among kin and those who have been accepted as blood-sworn brothers.
Roll Clan Name Meaning Notable
1 Borjigin Blue-eyed Imperial clan, Temujin's line

16.5 Complete Nomad Name Example
text
Trader Name: Temujin ("Iron Worker")
War Name: Temujin "Herd-Taker" (earned after capturing 100 horses)
Clan Name: Borjigin (Imperial lineage)
Full introduction to a stranger:
"I am Temujin. I have taken herds."
Full introduction to a trusted ally:
"I am Temujin of the Borjigin. The herd-taker. My father was Yesugei.
His father was Bartan. The line runs deep."
Full introduction to kin:
"Brother." (Clan name implied, never spoken to outsiders)

PART 16.9 — COMPLETE NAME GENERATION PROCESS
For each landed noble at world generation:
Step 1 — Determine Region and Culture
Use regional tables from Part 21
Step 2 — Generate Given Name
Roll 1d100 on appropriate regional table
If repeating a name within 3 generations of same house, add "the Younger/Elder"
or generate again
Step 3 — Generate House Name
Roll 1d100 on Geographic OR Legendary table (50/50 chance)
Can combine with regional feature: "of [Local Landmark]"
Step 4 — Optional Epithet
30% chance for starting epithet (young characters rarely have one)
Roll on Epithet table

-- 141 of 529 --

Epithet should reflect personality or known deed
Step 5 — Check for Duplicates
Compare with existing generated names in the region
If exact match (Given + House), modify:
Add "the Elder/Younger" if related
Add "of [Location]" if different holdings
Reroll given name if unrelated

BOOK III: CULTURAL SYSTEMS — COMPLETE RESTORATION
PART 17: WAR HORNS (Pages 88-91)
17.1 Core Principle
"When a horn calls, blades rise. Oaths are tested. In this world, the blood-
ground around them is covered in broken, discarded oaths. The horn remembers
what men forget."
Among landed peoples, the war horn is both instrument and symbol. A lord's
personal horn is passed down through generations, its voice known to all who owe
him fealty.
The horn calls men to battle, to feast, to mourning. A silent horn means a lord
has fallen — or his word has broken.
17.2 Horn Types
Horn Type Sound Used By Materials
Battle Horn Deep, urgent Lords, commanders Bronze, iron, aurochs horn
War Horn Long, rising Armies Bronze, wood, ox horn
Hunting Horn Quick, bright Hunters Cow horn
17.3 Horn Crafting
Horn Type Materials Cost (SD) Time
Hunting Horn Cow horn, leather wrap 10 3 days

-- 142 of 529 --

Battle Horn Bronze, iron bands 40 2 weeks
War Horn Bronze, wood, leather 60 1 month
Clan Horn Elk horn, carved 80 1 month
Oath-Horn Silver, ancient design 200 3 months
Mourning Horn Raw horn, unadorned 5 1 day
17.4 Horn Calls and Effects
Call Meaning Effect
One long blast Assembly All units within earshot gain +1 Initiative next
round
Two short blasts Attack +1 Morale on next Charge
Three short blasts Retreat -1 to enemy pursuit rolls
Rising then falling Lord's presence +1 Loyalty to all allied commanders
present
Single low note Mourning -1 Morale, +1 to vengeance rolls next battle
Repeated pattern Recognition Enemy must check morale if they recognize the horn
17.5 The Horn of Oaths
A lord's personal horn is more than a tool — it is a symbol of his word. When a
lord swears an oath, he does so with hand on his horn. When he keeps it, the
horn's voice carries truth. When he breaks it, the horn is silenced.
Oath-Breaker's Horn: If a lord breaks a sworn oath, his horn cannot be used
until he atones. Attempts to sound it produce only a dull, dead note. All who
hear know what it means.
Horn Breaking: To destroy an enemy's horn in battle is a great shame. The horn-
breaker gains +1 Reputation (Cruel or Mighty, depending on context). The horn's
owner loses -1 Reputation and -1 Loyalty from all who witnessed it.
Horn as Trophy: Capturing an enemy lord's horn grants +1 Reputation. Returning
it (for ransom or as gesture) grants +2 Opinion with that lord's house.
PART 18: SMUDGING & SHARING CIRCLE (Pages 91-95)
18.1 Core Principle
Smudging is the oldest prayer. Before any ceremony that matters, before any
gathering that means something, before grief is spoken or joy is shared — there
is the smoke.
The smoke carries what needs to leave — anger, grief, fear, the weight of
unkindness. It does not destroy these things. It lifts them, transforms them,
returns them to the air where they can become something else.
18.2 Smudging Materials
Material Source Cost (SD) Uses
Sage (bundle) Eastern Forests, Plains 3 Cleansing, purification
Cedar (bundle) Eastern Forests 4 Protection, prayers
Sweetgrass Plains, Meadows 5 Kindness, healing
Tobacco (loose) Trade, Southern 2 Offering, gratitude
18.3 The Sharing Circle
When people gather for any purpose — grief, celebration, council, or simply to
catch up — they sit in a circle. The circle has no beginning and no end. No one
sits at the head. No one is above another. All are equal within it.
The circle moves clockwise, following the sun's path across the sky. This is the
way of things — the way the world turns, the way time moves, the way prayers
rise.
18.4 The Talking Stick
An object is placed in the center — a carved stick, a feather, a stone. When the
circle begins, the object is passed clockwise. Whoever holds it speaks. No one
else speaks. No one interrupts. No one prepares their response while another
talks. They simply listen.
18.5 The Seven Smudging Gestures
A helper sits to the right of the elder. He tends the smudge bowl, keeping the

-- 143 of 529 --

plants burning, never blowing on the smoke — only fanning it with an eagle
feather, encouraging it to rise.
As the bowl passes, each person smudges themselves in the old way:
Gesture Words Meaning
Eyes "To see good things" To witness the world with kindness, to see beauty
even in hardship
Ears "To hear good things" To listen deeply, to hear the truth beneath words
Heart "To love openly" To keep the heart soft, to love even when it hurts
Mouth/Breath "To speak good things" To let words carry goodness, to speak
truth with care
Head "To have good thoughts" To think clearly, to hold good intentions
Legs/Feet "To walk a good path" To move through the world with purpose, to
leave good footprints
Whole Body "To carry goodness" To carry goodness with you wherever you go
18.6 When They Gather
Occasion Purpose
Grief To let the smoke carry what is too heavy to hold alone
Council To speak clearly and listen deeply before decisions
Celebration To give thanks, to witness joy together
Conflict To cool anger, to remember they are still family
Weekly Gathering To catch up, to be seen, to know they are not alone
Before Ceremony To prepare the spirit for what comes
18.7 Smoke Rising as Prayer
As the smoke rises, so does prayer. The smoke carries words, intentions, grief,
hope — lifting them up. This is why smudging is done before speaking, before
asking, before offering thanks. The smoke prepares the way.
18.8 Mechanical Effects
Situation Effect
Before a ceremony +1 to ceremony effectiveness (the mind is clear)
Before a difficult conversation +1 to diplomacy (intention is set)
18.9 Individual Smudging
A person may smudge alone, in their home, at dawn or dusk. The same is true —
the smoke rises, the prayer rises. This is not less powerful than group
ceremony. It is simply private.
"The smoke does not ask if you are worthy. It simply rises. Your prayer rises
with it. That is enough."
— Elder Wisdom
PART 19: TRIBAL FAMILY SYSTEMS (Pages 95-99 + Patch 7.1)
19.1 Core Principle
Among tribal and nomadic peoples, the family is the voice of the tribe. Not
chiefs. Not elders. Families. Because kindness is learned at the hearth, at the
fire, in the small moments between parent and child.
19.2 Family Reputation (Per Patch 7.1)
Each family has a reputation within the tribe. This affects how they are
treated, how their word is received, and how much influence they carry.
Roll 1d6 Reputation Effect Wealth Food Special
1 Selfish -1 to tribe loyalty, hoards resources 1.5x wealth Poor
(0.5x FSU) Hated by neighbors
2 Struggling Trying but failing, may need help 0.5x wealth Barely
surviving Refugees may be turned away
3 Neutral No strong reputation 1.0x wealth Adequate —
4 Generous +1 to trade with other families 0.8x wealth (gave away)
Plentiful (1.5x FSU) Loved by neighbors
5 Honored Known for kindness, +1 to diplomacy 1.2x wealth Plentiful +1
to diplomacy
6 Legendary Stories told about them, +2 to all family interactions
2.0x wealth Abundant May adopt worthy strangers

-- 144 of 529 --

19.3 Family Loyalty
Each family has a loyalty score to the chief (0-6). This determines how
willingly they follow, how much they contribute, and whether they might rebel.
Score Meaning
0-1 Resentful, may rebel
2-3 Neutral, follow but don't volunteer
4-5 Loyal, contribute willingly
6 Devoted, would die for the chief
19.4 Family Council
When the chief makes a decision that affects the whole tribe, the families have
a voice:
Situation Families React
Chief decides to go to war Families with warriors in danger may object
Chief accepts refugees Families with spare food may support or resist
Chief makes trade agreement Families who produce goods are affected
Chief marries outside tribe Families lose/gain alliances
Chief fails to share scarce food Families remember
Resolution: Roll 2d6 + Chief's Reputation with Families
Roll Outcome
2-4 Families openly oppose. Loyalty -2, possible rebellion
5-7 Families grumble but comply. Loyalty -1
8-10 Families support. Loyalty +1
11-12 Families embrace decision. Loyalty +2, offer additional support
19.5 Hospitality and The Stranger
Among tribal peoples, hospitality is sacred. A stranger who comes to the fire
must be fed before they are asked their name. This is not kindness — it is
survival. The stranger today may be the ally tomorrow. The stranger turned away
may be the enemy next season.
The Stranger at Your Fire:
When refugees arrive at a tribal camp, the families decide:
Family Reputation Response
Selfish (1) Turn refugees away (-1 tribe loyalty, refugees remember)
Struggling (2) Want to help but can't — send refugees to next camp
Neutral (3) Debate. Roll family council.
Generous (4) Accept refugees, share food (-1 FSU, +1 tribe reputation)
Honored (5) Welcome refugees, give generously (-2 FSU, +2 reputation)
Legendary (6) Adopt refugees as family (-3 FSU, +3 reputation, possible new
family member)
PART 20: THE EIGHT TRADITIONS (Pages 99-100)
"Some men are crowned not because they lift the crown to their head, but because
other men place it there and bend." The old king wore eight crowns — one for
each tradition, united in a single head. When he died, they did not break. They
simply returned to their eight peoples. Now they wait for someone who can gather
them again.
PART 21: CROWN OF BLOOD — CENTRAL PLAINS (Pages 100-102 + Patch 7.4)
21.1 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as War King if they:
Win 3+ major battles in the Central Plains
Defeat the previous War King in battle
Command the loyalty of 5+ great houses of the Plains
21.2 The Iron Seat
The throne of the Central Plains is called the Iron Seat. It has killed more
kings than age — not through treachery, but through the simple truth that those

-- 145 of 529 --

who sit in it must be strong enough to hold it. Every king knows: the Seat
remembers. It does not miss them when they fall.
21.3 War King Legitimacy Track
Battles Won Legitimacy Score
1 1 (aspirant)
2 2 (contender)
3 3 (recognized in home region)
4 4 (known abroad)
5+ 5 (undeniable)
21.4 Challenge Mechanics
Any lord with an equal or greater War King score may challenge the sitting king.
The challenge must be public. Refusal is seen as weakness and reduces Opinion by
-2 with all houses. If the challenge is accepted, the two armies meet — or the
two kings meet in single combat, if both agree.
21.5 No Inheritance
Children do not inherit the War Kingship. They must win it themselves. A son of
a king starts with +1 Reputation (his name is known) but no automatic claim.
21.6 The Iron Seat's Domain (Per Patch 7.4)
The Crown of Blood does not rule the entire region — it rules the heart of it.
Even the War King's direct domain is only 200 miles across. Beyond that lie
march lords, border counts, and wild lands where his word is suggestion, not
law.
Travel Time Across War King's Direct Domain: 2-3 weeks
PART 22: CROWN OF CONTRACTS — WESTERN RIVERS (Pages 102-104 + Patch 7.4)
22.1 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as Charter King if they:
Secure trade agreements with 75% of major river holdings
Are elected by the River Council
Receive the signed charter of participating lords
22.2 The Toll Throne
The throne of the Western Rivers is called the Toll Throne. It was paid for
grain by grain, tariff by tariff, toll by toll. Every king who sits in it knows:
they have earned it. The throne expects to be earned anew each generation.
22.3 River Council Mechanics
The River Council meets when a king dies or when a claimant demands recognition.
It uses the Grand Ball framework for attendance and influence. Each attending
lord may vote. Voting is not by ballot but by signed charter — a lord who
supports a claimant adds their name and seal to the charter.
Council Attendance Roll: 2d6 + Relations modifiers
Roll Outcome
2-4 Few attend (legitimacy questioned)
5-8 Moderate attendance
9-11 Nearly all attend (strong mandate)
12 Every lord attends (unprecedented)
22.4 Breach of Charter
If a king violates the terms of his charter (raising tolls without consent,
seizing merchant goods, breaking trade agreements), any signatory lord may
withdraw their support. If enough lords withdraw (more than half), the king is
considered to have broken the contract and may be deposed.
22.5 Succession by Election
When a king dies, the River Council elects a new king. Any lord may be

-- 146 of 529 --

considered, regardless of blood. Merchant princes with sufficient wealth may
also stand — silver speaks loudly in the rivers.
22.6 The Toll Throne's Rivers (Per Patch 7.4)
The Western Rivers region has 3 major river systems, each 300-500 miles long. A
merchant traveling from the headwaters to the delta:
Downstream: 3-4 weeks
Upstream (poling/towing): 6-8 weeks
PART 23: CROWN OF ROOTS — EASTERN FORESTS (TRIBAL) (Pages 104-106 + Patch 7.4)
23.1 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as Sachem if they:
Are chosen by the Forest Council of elders
Show signs of acceptance from the land
Are recognized by the people as one who carries their will
23.2 The Living Stool
The throne of the tribal forests is not a throne at all — it is a living stump,
still growing, still rooted in the earth. The Sachem sits on it not to rule but
to listen — to the land, to the people, to the ancestors who speak through the
roots.
23.3 Forest Council Mechanics
The Forest Council consists of elders from each family. They do not vote — they
speak until consensus emerges.
Council Size Time to Consensus
Small tribe (3-4 families) 1-3 days
Medium tribe (5-7 families) 3-7 days
Large tribe (8+ families) 7-14 days
23.4 Walking the Land Ceremony
A claimant must walk the sacred places of the forest: ancient groves, hidden
springs, places where the veil between worlds is thin. They walk alone, with
only their knowledge of the land to guide them.
If they complete the walk, they return. If they do not, the forest has taken
them — and they were not meant to be Sachem.
23.5 Signs of Acceptance
The land shows its acceptance through signs:
Game appears where there was none
Water flows from springs long dry
Trees bloom out of season
The wind carries whispers of approval
These are not magical — they are natural phenomena that the people interpret as
signs. Whether the spirits truly speak or the people simply believe, the effect
is the same.
23.6 Land's Rejection
If a Sachem acts against the forest — allowing sacred groves to be cut,
permitting overhunting, ignoring the elders' counsel — the signs reverse. Game
grows scarce. Springs dry. The wind carries warnings. Loyalty drops. The people
begin to whisper that the Sachem has lost the land's favor.

-- 147 of 529 --

23.7 The Living Stool's Forest (Per Patch 7.4)
The Eastern Forests are 600 miles north-south. A messenger traveling from the
northern tribal camps to the southern settled hill forts:
6-8 weeks in summer
Impossible in winter (deep snow, frozen rivers)
PART 24: CROWN OF THE GREEN DRAKE — EASTERN FORESTS (SETTLED) (Pages 106-109)
24.1 The Green Drake Myth
In the oldest songs, a dragon threatened to swallow the world. A man — called
the Green Drake, though he was no dragon — fought it alone. He killed it, but
lost his leg in the battle.
The spirits, moved by his sacrifice, raised the Eastern Forest around him. The
forest brought rivers, valleys, animals, rain, sun — the whole world came to
him. When he died, leaning against a birch, his last breath became the eastern
wind, which still whispers the history of the people to those who listen and
believe. "The wind is his breath. The forest is his gift. We are his children.
This is not a story. This is truth."
24.2 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as Lord of the High Mound if they:
Carry the blood of the Green Drake (can be traced, however distantly)
Prove skill with the bow in the Great Hunt
Are whispered of by the wind (high reputation among the folk)
24.3 The Three Signs
Sign Evidence Requirement
Blood Descent from the Green Drake Can be traced, even distantly
Bow Proven hunting skill Must have taken a great beast in fair chase
Whisper The wind speaks of you Reputation ≥ 6, Opinion ≥ +2 across multiple
forest lords
24.4 Hill Forts as Seats
The settled folk do not rule from caves or stumps. They build hill forts — earth
and timber, ditches and banks, placed on high ground where the wind speaks
loudest. A hill fort is not just a home; it is an ear to the whispers.
Hill Fort Upgrades:
Upgrade Cost (SD) Time Effect
The High Mound 200 SD 1 year +1 to all ceremonial rolls
The Bowyer's Grove 150 SD 6 months +1 to hunting, can train elite
archers
The Hunting Lodge 100 SD 3 months +1 to hunting, young lords train
faster
24.5 The Great Hunt
When a king dies, the claimants hunt. They enter the forest alone, with only
their bow and their skill. They hunt for three days. The one who returns with
the greatest kill, who shows the most respect to the animal's spirit, who the
folk see as worthy — he becomes king.
Hunt Result Outcome
No kill Not worthy — cannot be king
Small game (rabbit, fowl) Minimal respect, unlikely to win
Medium game (deer, boar) Respectable, possible contender
Great beast (stag, bear, wolf) Strong claim
24.6 The Whisper Test
Before a king is confirmed, he must spend a night alone at the Green Drake's
Birch (where the first forest grew). He must listen to the wind. If he hears
nothing, he is not ready. If he hears only his own fears, he is not worthy. If

-- 148 of 529 --

he hears the whispers of his ancestors, of the Green Drake himself, he may
return and be crowned.
24.7 Relations with Tribal Folk
The settled and tribal peoples of the forest have traded, fought, and
intermarried for centuries. They know each other.
Relation Opinion Modifier
Trade partners +2 base
Intermarried families +3 base
Recent conflict -2 for 1 generation
Shared ceremony attendance +1 for 1 year
PART 25: CROWN OF STONE — SOUTHERN MOUNTAINS (Pages 109-111 + Patch 7.4)
25.1 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as Blood King only through direct bloodline
inheritance. The crown passes from father to son, mother to daughter, generation
to generation.
25.2 The Stone Seat
The throne of the Southern Mountains is carved from the mountain itself. It has
never been moved. It has waited for the right blood to sit in it for longer than
any can remember. It can wait again.
25.3 Ancestor Recognition
A new king must visit the ancestral tombs carved into the mountain's heart. They
walk among the bones of those who came before. If the ancestors accept them,
they feel... something. A presence. A peace. If the ancestors reject them, the
mountain feels empty, cold, unwelcoming.
25.4 Blood Purity
The mountain folk track blood purity — how closely related a claimant is to the
previous kings. This is not about racism; it is about tradition. The mountain
remembers names, not faces.
Relation Purity Score
Direct child 10
Grandchild 8
Sibling 7
Niece/nephew 5
Distant relation 3
A claimant with purity score 5+ is considered "of the blood." Below that, they
are considered outsiders and cannot inherit — they must conquer.
25.5 Usurper Mechanics
Someone not of the blood can only rule by conquest. They may take the Stone Seat
by force, but they will never have full legitimacy. They suffer:
-2 Opinion from all mountain houses
Constant rebellion risk
No inheritance — their children are not considered blood
25.6 The Stone Seat's Mountains (Per Patch 7.4)
The Southern Mountains are a labyrinth of ranges and valleys. A lord traveling
from one end of the region to the other:
Using known passes: 6-8 weeks
Exploring new routes: 3-4 months, with high risk
PART 26: CROWN OF THE NORTH WIND — NORTHERN SNOWLANDS (Per Patch 34.0/35.0 —
Scottish Clans & Norman Lords)
26.1 Cultural Identity

-- 149 of 529 --

The northern lords are a unique mixture:
Highland clan roots (fierce family loyalty, blood feuds that last generations)
Norman conqueror pragmatism (they took this land by the sword and they hold it
by the sword, but they also administer it, build on it, rule it)
They are welcoming but judging. A northern lord will share his fire, his food,
his ale with any traveler. He will also watch you all night to see if you're
worth the warmth.
26.2 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as Lord of the High Seat if they satisfy at least
two of the Threefold Right:
Right Evidence
Blood Claim to the line (even distant)
Steel 3+ major victories OR conquest of key holding
Word No proven oathbreaking; at least one major oath kept at personal cost
26.3 The High Seat
The throne of the northern snowlands is not a throne. It is the highest chair in
the great hall — often just a larger version of every other chair, placed on a
slight rise in the floor. The king sits there because everyone else chooses to
sit lower.
When a king dies, the chair remains empty until the Gathering chooses a new
king. The lords sit in a circle around it, facing each other.
26.4 The Gathering
When a king dies, the lords come to the High Seat. There is no fixed schedule —
they come when they can.
Step Description
The Speaking Each lord speaks what they know of the claimants. Truth is
expected.
The Standing Claimants stand and give their word — what they will protect,
what they will sacrifice.
The Choosing Lords step forward and stand behind the one they choose.
The Acclamation The claimant with the most lords behind them is king.
26.5 Northern Speech
A Northerner Says... A Southerner Hears... What It Actually Means
"That's a bold plan." "He approves." "That plan might kill us all, but I'm
willing to watch."
"You talk well." "He respects my eloquence." "You talk too much. Show me what
you can do."
"Sit by the fire." "He welcomes me." "I'm watching you. The fire makes it
easier."
"We'll see." "He's considering it." "No. But I have reasons for not saying
no directly."
(Silence) "He has nothing to say." "I have much to say. None of it is for
your ears."
26.6 Strategic Silence
Northern lords are not fools. They know when truth serves and when it endangers.
If their child is a ward in a southern court, they will smile at insults, nod at
foolishness, and wait. They will not trade their child's safety for the
satisfaction of being right.
26.7 The Ward's Silence
When a northern lord has a child serving as ward in another lord's court:
Situation Expected Response Mechanical Effect
Insult at court Smile, nod, say nothing +1 to Patience (tracked internally)
Child treated poorly Observe, document, wait -1 Opinion (hidden), +2 to future
vengeance

-- 150 of 529 --

Child endangered Immediate action, silence broken Risk of feud, but child
comes first
Wardship ends Lord collects child, rides north All stored insults
available for response
26.8 Survival is the Only Law
A king who cannot keep his people alive through winter has no right to rule.
Full stop. This is not spoken — it is known. When a king fails, the lords do not
vote him out. They simply stop coming when he calls.
26.9 The High Seat's Snowlands (Per Patch 7.4)
The Northern Snowlands are the most sparsely populated. A journey from Harald's
Hold in the west to the eastern fishing villages:
Summer: 5-6 weeks
Winter: 3-4 months (if possible at all)
PART 27: THE FELT CROWN — NOMAD STEPPE (TRADITIONAL) (Pages 115-117)
27.1 Kingship Triggers
A claimant may be recognized as Khan if they:
Unite 75%+ of steppe clans as vassals
Are confirmed by Kurultai
Are lifted on the felt by clan chiefs
27.2 The Felt Crown
The crown of the steppe is not gold or stone — it is a felt blanket. When the
clan chiefs lift you on the felt, they are not crowning you. They are saying:
"You are light enough to carry. Now carry us."
A Khan who forgets this becomes heavy. Heavy Khans fall.
27.3 Kurultai Confirmation
The Kurultai is a gathering of all clan chiefs at a sacred ground. They debate,
they argue, they drink fermented mare's milk, and eventually they decide. The
claimant must have the support of at least two-thirds of the chiefs to be
confirmed.
Kurultai Outcome Result
Unanimous support Khan's authority unchallenged, +2 Loyalty all clans
Two-thirds support Khan confirmed, but opposition clans resentful (-1
Loyalty)
Bare majority Khan confirmed but weak, constant challenges
27.4 Mobile Throne
A Khan does not sit on a throne. They sit on a horse. The throne moves. The
Khan's authority travels with the Orda (royal camp), which moves with the
seasons, with the herds, with the grass.
27.5 Succession by Kurultai
When a Khan dies, the Kurultai gathers again. Blood matters, but so does
ability. A son may be chosen — or a brother, a cousin, a completely different
clan chief if he has proven himself worthy. The chiefs choose the one most
likely to keep the horde united.
PART 28: THE SETTLED CROWN — NOMAD STEPPE (TRANSFORMED) (Pages 117-120)
28.1 Transformation Trigger
A Khan may begin the transformation to settled rule if they:
Control at least one city or castle for 5+ years
Establish a permanent capital

-- 151 of 529 --

Begin using settled administration
28.2 Cultural Conversion Stages
Stage Time Effect Resistance
Conquest Year 1 Khan rules settled land but lives in Orda High (clans
suspicious)
Tribute Year 2-3 Khan collects taxes, uses scribes Moderate
Integration Year 4-5 Khan's children educated both ways Low
Transformation Year 6+ Khan holds both titles, rules from palace Minimal
28.3 Conversion Tools
Tool Mechanics Cultural Impact
Economic Ties Trade agreements, tribute Settled goods become desirable
Political Marriage Marriage alliances Families blend, loyalties blur
Religious Syncretism Blend faiths Spiritual unity, less resistance
28.4 The Cost of Transformation
Loss Effect
Traditionalist clans break away -X loyalty, possible rebellion
Steppe identity dilutes Future generations may not remember the old ways
Settled lords still suspicious Never fully trusted by either world
The Felt Crown feels heavier Khan now carries two peoples, not one
28.5 What They Become
Region Title for Transformed Khan Meaning
Central Plains "The Horse Lord" Respectful, wary — he came on a horse, now
he stays
Western Rivers "The Tribute Khan" Mercantile — he collects taxes now,
like us
Eastern Forests "The Two-Path One" Curious — he walks both ways
Southern Mountains "The Usurper" Dismissive — no blood, no crown
Northern Snowlands "The Survivor" Practical — he adapted. We respect
that.
Steppe (traditionalists) "The Lost One" Tragic — he forgot the grass
PART 29: THE RUBICON CROWN — ARMY PROCLAMATION (Pages 120-123)
29.1 Core Principle
This is the moment when an army, through a combination of loyalty, victories,
charismatic leadership, and careful nudges, decides to proclaim their commander
king — regardless of what any other lord, council, or tradition says.
29.2 The Rubicon Threshold
An army does not proclaim a king lightly. They must be ready:
Factor Requirement Contribution
Loyalty Average commander loyalty ≥ 4 +1 per point above 3
Victories 5+ major battles won +1 per 2 victories
Morale Army morale "Exalted" for 3+ weeks +1 per week
Banner Tier Commander's personal banner Tier 4+ +2
Reputation Commander's reputation ≥ 7 +2
War Will Army War Will ≥ 8 +1
Shared Sacrifice Army has endured a near-disaster together +2 (once)
The Whisper Leader has planted the thought +1 to +3
The Rubicon Score = Sum of all factors.
Threshold: 12+
29.3 The Whisper — Planting the Thought
A leader who wants to be proclaimed king cannot simply ask. They must plant the
thought:
Technique Effect
Victory Speeches over multiple battles +1
Leading from the front, sharing hardship +1
Talking of "what comes after" +1
"When I am king..." in private conversations +1
29.4 The Crossing — Moment of Proclamation
When the Rubicon Score reaches 12, the moment can happen. The leader may choose
to let it happen or suppress it.

-- 152 of 529 --

If the leader does nothing: The army acts. Roll 2d6
Roll The Proclamation
2-4 Hesitant — "Some of us have been talking..."
5-8 Firm — "The men have decided. You are our king."
9-11 Overwhelming — Entire army shouts as one
12 Legendary — They crown you on the spot with whatever is at hand
If the leader refuses: Roll 2d6 + Rubicon Score
Roll Outcome
≤ 8 They accept. But something breaks. -2 Loyalty
9-11 They argue. Loyalty split.
12+ They refuse your refusal. "You are our king whether you will it or not."
29.5 Aftermath
Change Effect
All proclaiming units Loyalty becomes 6 (fanatic) permanently
Morale +2 Morale for all proclaiming units
Recruitment +1d6x10 recruits per season
Reputation +3 Reputation, possible new nickname
Other lords Opinion -2 with all non-proclaiming lords
Other kings Relations drop to -3 (rival claimants)
Former liege If any, they are now enemy
29.6 The Rubicon King's Legitimacy
A Rubicon King has no legitimacy by any traditional crown. Their authority rests
on:
The army that proclaimed them
Fear (other lords may bend to avoid being crushed)
Time (if they rule long enough, people forget how it started)
Marriage into a legitimate line
Conquest of a capital (adds Crown of Blood)
Recognition by an existing king
PART 30: THE BENDING (Pages 123-125 + Patch 7.3)
30.1 The Bend Defined
The Bend is the formal act of swearing fealty to a regional king. It is both
ceremony and substance — a lord kneels, speaks his oath, and rises a vassal.
30.2 Bending Triggers
A lord may choose to bend when:
Opinion of the king ≥ +2
Fear (army at gates, no hope of victory)
Mutual benefit (trade, protection, alliance)
30.3 Public Ceremony
The bending ceremony is a public act. The lord kneels, places his hands between
the king's hands, and speaks the oath:
"I, [name] of [house], do swear fealty to [king name], King of [crown]. I shall
be his man, in winter and summer, in war and peace, in life and death. This I
swear by my sword, my land, and the honor of my house. So hear me."
30.4 Reluctant Bend
If a lord bends only out of fear, the oath may be spoken through clenched teeth.
The king knows. The crowd knows. The lord knows. But the knee is bent, and the

-- 153 of 529 --

oath is spoken. That is enough — for now.
Mechanical Effect: Loyalty starts at 1 instead of 3. Must be earned.
30.5 Broken Bend
If a vassal rebels, they are said to have "broken their bend." This carries:
-3 Reputation (oathbreaker)
Former liege gains Claim
All other lords -1 Opinion (if you'll betray him, you'll betray me)
30.6 Multiple Bends
A lord may bend to multiple kings? No. This is not recognized. A lord who tries
to serve two kings serves none. Any king who discovers a vassal has bent
elsewhere may:
Demand they renounce the other
Seize lands
Declare war
30.7 The Feudal Web at Game Start (Per Patch 7.3)
At campaign start, the feudal web looks like this:
text
Level 0: THE EMPTY THRONE
├── 16 Great Lords (sworn to crown, now independent in practice)
│ ├── 80 Minor Lords (sworn to Great Lords)
│ │ ├── 148 Landed Knights (sworn to Minor Lords)
│ │ └── (indirectly, also sworn to Great Lords)
│ └── 192 Landed Knights (directly sworn to Great Lords)
│
└── 27 Independent Minor Lords (sworn to no Great Lord)
├── 148 Landed Knights (sworn to Independent Minor Lords)
└── (these Minor Lords owe fealty only to the Empty Throne)
Total Oaths:
16 Great Lords (theoretically sworn to crown)
107 Minor Lords (80 vassal + 27 independent)
340 Landed Knights (148 + 192)
463 total noble characters with defined liege relationships
PART 31: THE EMPTY THRONE (Pages 125-127)
31.1 The Old King's Crown
The last king to wear all eight crowns died thirty years ago. He was old, wise,
and loved. When he passed, his children had predeceased him, his nephews were
distant, and his cousins were many. No one could agree who should rule. So no
one ruled.
The eight crowns returned to their eight peoples. The throne sat empty. The
realm learned to live without a king.
31.2 The Hidden Heir
Some say a daughter survived. Hidden away in the mountains, raised in secret by
those who remembered what loyalty meant. A girl with the old king's eyes, they
whisper. A woman now, waiting.
Others say she died with the rest. That the line is truly broken. That the

-- 154 of 529 --

throne will never be filled again.
Both cannot be true. Both might be lies. The truth waits in the Southern
Mountains, in a fortress no map shows, guarded by knights who have kept their
oath for thirty years.
31.3 The Great Reconciliation
If all eight crowns are gathered on one head, the Empty Throne is filled. The
one who wears them may call themselves High King — but only if:
They hold all eight crowns
They are recognized by at least half the great houses of each region
They can defend the throne against all challengers
This has not happened since the old king died. It may never happen again.

37.1 Core Rule
Trigger: The moment a unit satisfies the Tier requirement...
Resolution: Immediately resolve the Tier Upgrade before the next action, scene,
or battle round. Tiering does not require:
Player choice
Commander approval
Rest
Narrative justification
Growth interrupts the war.
37.2 Tier Requirements
Upgrade Requirement
1 → 2 Unit survives 3 battles
2 → 3 Unit survives 6 battles
3 → 4 Unit survives 10 battles
4 → 5 Unit survives 15 battles
"Survives" = not reduced to 0 people.
37.3 Tier Benefits
Tier Benefits
Tier 2 +1 Initiative, Morale DC -1
Tier 3 +1 Weapon Die (permanent), +1 Loyalty bonus to allied commanders
Tier 4 +1 Armor Class, Morale DC -1
Tier 5 Choose one (locked permanently): +1d6 on all attacks / Immune to
first Morale check / +1 AC while Defending

PART 39: COMMAND CAPACITY — EXPANDED (Pages 139-142)
39.1 Core Principle
A commander's ability to lead is not merely a matter of logistics — it is a
matter of presence, reputation, and the loyalty they inspire. A great name can
rally thousands where a lesser lord could barely hold a hundred. Command
Capacity represents how many soldiers a commander can effectively control in
battle. Exceed this limit, and even the greatest lord will find their orders
lost in the chaos.
39.2 Base Command Capacity
Each commander can effectively control troops based on their personal Tier:
Commander Tier Base Capacity
Tier 1 100 troops
Tier 2 250 troops
Tier 3 500 troops
Tier 4 1,000 troops
Tier 5 2,000 troops
39.3 Banner Tier Multiplier
A commander's Banner Tier multiplies their Base Capacity, representing the
renown and recognition that draws soldiers to follow their banner:
Banner Tier Capacity Multiplier
Tier 1 Banner x1 (no multiplier)
Tier 2 Banner x1.5 (round up)
Tier 3 Banner x2
Tier 4 Banner x3
Tier 5 Banner x4
39.4 Final Command Capacity Formula
text
Final Command Capacity = Base Capacity × Banner Multiplier
Examples:

-- 158 of 529 --

Commander Tier Banner Tier Base Capacity Multiplier Final Capacity
Tier 1 Tier 1 100 x1 100 troops
Tier 2 Tier 2 250 x1.5 375 troops
Tier 3 Tier 3 500 x2 1,000 troops
Tier 3 Tier 4 500 x3 1,500 troops
Tier 4 Tier 3 1,000 x2 2,000 troops
Tier 4 Tier 4 1,000 x3 3,000 troops
Tier 5 Tier 5 2,000 x4 8,000 troops
39.5 Command Capacity by Tier (Quick Reference Table)
Commander Tier Banner Tier 1 Banner Tier 2 Banner Tier 3 Banner
Tier 4 Banner Tier 5
Tier 1 100 150 200 300 400
Tier 2 250 375 500 750 1,000
Tier 3 500 750 1,000 1,500 2,000
Tier 4 1,000 1,500 2,000 3,000 4,000
Tier 5 2,000 3,000 4,000 6,000 8,000
39.6 Effects of Exceeding Command Capacity
If a commander leads more troops than their Command Capacity:
Excess Percentage Penalty
1-25% over -1 to morale checks, -1 to battle maneuver rolls
26-50% over -2 to morale checks, -2 to battle maneuver rolls, orders delayed
51-75% over -3 to morale checks, cannot use special tactics, desertion risk
76%+ over Automatic morale failure, unit may refuse orders, chaos in ranks
39.7 Multiple Units Under One Commander
A commander's capacity applies to ALL units under their direct command in a
battle. They may split their capacity across multiple units.
39.8 Increasing Command Capacity
Method Effect
Increasing Commander Tier Increases Base Capacity
Increasing Banner Tier Increases Multiplier
Legendary Reputation (8+) +10% to final capacity (stacks)
War Hero status +5% to final capacity

40.1 Core Principle
The player does not "buy units." They attract commanders and build forces
through reputation, loyalty, and deeds.

Commander is famous +1 DC

Drill Morale (ignore first Morale check)
40.6 The Equipment Reality — Nothing Spawns (Per Patch 7.7)
A lord cannot simply say "I equip 100 men with plate armor" and deduct coin from
treasury. Plate armor must exist in the world. It must be:
Crafted in a holding with appropriate facilities
Purchased from a holding that has it in stock
Looted from a defeated enemy
Inherited from previous generations
Received as tribute or gift
The Golden Rule: If it isn't in a holding's inventory, it isn't available for
purchase.

40.8 Equipment Acquisition Options
Option Requirements
Craft It Facility, raw materials, skilled labor, time
Buy It Locally Check holding inventory, pay market price
Import It Send caravan, pay purchase + transport + risk
Loot It Defeat enemies, roll on loot tables
Requisition It (Vassals) Demand from vassal knights, requires loyalty check
40.9 The Plate Armor Rule
To equip a single soldier with plate armor:
Requirement Detail
Raw Materials 30 iron (Common) or 25 iron (High-Grade) or 20 iron (Superb)
Crafting Time 6 weeks per suit (master armorer)
Facility Advanced Forge (requires City or Great Lord's keep)
Master Armorer 1 per 5 suits being crafted simultaneously
Cost (if buying) 50 SD base × quality × demand
Market Reality:
A typical City might have 10-20 plate suits in inventory
A Town will have 0-2 (imported)
A Village will have 0

40.13 Mercenary Bands as Persistent Factions (Per Patch 7.11)
Mercenary bands are not temporary units. They are persistent factions that exist
in the world whether hired or not. They have:
A name and reputation
A commander with personality and ambitions
A core strength (number of veterans)
A home base or region of operation
A treasury (their own money)
A contract history (who they've fought for)
A grudge list (who wronged them)

40.15 Mercenary Loyalty & Satisfaction
Mercenaries have a Satisfaction Score (1-10) that determines if they will:
Renew their contract
Fight effectively
Abandon you in battle
Switch sides for better pay
Base Satisfaction = 5 + (Pay Quality) + (Casualty Modifier) + (Historical
Modifier)
Factor Modifier
Pay is fair (market rate) +1
Pay is generous (+25% above market) +2
Pay is low (-25% below market) -2
Pay is insulting (-50% below) -4
Light casualties (<10% in last battle) +1
Moderate casualties (10-25%) 0
Heavy casualties (25-50%) -2
Devastating casualties (>50%) -4
Promised bonus paid +2
Promised bonus withheld -4
Commander treated well +1
Commander treated poorly -2
Previous positive history +1 per job
Previous negative history -2 per grudge
Satisfaction Thresholds:
Score Behavior
9-10 Fanatically loyal — will fight to the death, accept lower pay next time
7-8 Satisfied — fight well, likely to renew
5-6 Neutral — do the job, no more
3-4 Unhappy — may demand renegotiation, fight poorly (-1 morale)
1-2 Angry — looking for excuse to leave, may desert
0 or less Betrayal — will switch sides for any offer, may loot your lands on
way out
PART 41: UNIVERSAL RECRUITMENT (Per Patch 6.1, 7.7)
41.1 Core Principle
Anyone can recruit fighters. Training and equipment determine effectiveness.
Recruitment gathers people, while arming them costs silver.
41.2 Recruitment Sources
All recruitment draws from people physically present. Recruitment Pool = Local
Free Men + Armed Refugees
41.3 Refugee Militia Unit
Refugee fighters are functional but weak. Typical traits:
Poor Equipment
Low Morale

-- 163 of 529 --

Untrained
They can still fight, raid, or defend settlements
41.4 Recruitment Rule
Once armed, refugees join the recruitment pool as fighters.
Unarmed Refugees → pay silver → Armed Refugees → Recruitable
41.5 Landless Recruitment
Landless characters recruit from the local recruitment pool.
41.6 Weekly Replenishment
Units replace losses from the same recruitment pool.
Weekly Replenishment Limit: 10 soldiers per unit
41.7 Refugee Creation
Refugees are created when regions destabilize. Possible causes:
Settlement destroyed
Army defeated
Occupation
Famine
41.8 Strategic Tradeoff
Arming refugees costs silver but expands manpower.
Example decision:
Spend 60 silver → build trade caravan
OR Spend 60 silver → arm 20 refugees
Players must choose between economy or manpower.
41.9 The Equipment Check (Per Patch 7.7)
Before a unit can be recruited, the player must:
Check Inventory: Does the holding have the required weapons and armor?
If Yes: Proceed with recruitment, remove items from inventory
If No: Either:
Choose different equipment (if available)
Wait for items to be crafted or imported
Send caravan to acquire items elsewhere
Recruit fewer soldiers (only as many as can be equipped)
Example:
Lord wants to recruit 100 soldiers with swords and chain.
Holding inventory: 45 swords, 30 chain suits
Can equip: 30 soldiers now
Must acquire: 70 more swords, 70 more chain suits
Options: Craft (weeks), Import (weeks), or recruit in phases
41.10 Phased Recruitment
A lord may recruit in phases as equipment becomes available.

-- 164 of 529 --

Week Action
Week 1 Craft 20 swords, 20 chain suits → equip 20 soldiers
Week 2 Craft 20 more swords, 20 more chain suits → equip 20 more
Week 3 Caravan arrives with 30 swords, 30 chain suits → equip final 60
Week 4 Unit of 100 soldiers fully formed
Total time: 1 month from decision to fielding
41.11 Material Sourcing (Artificer)
Method How It Works Risk/Reward
Buy Locally Purchase from holding inventory Reliable, may be expensive
Trade with Caravans Order through merchants Can get rare materials, takes
time
Mine Yourself Spend time gathering Free materials, time-consuming
Barter Trade finished items for materials Good for building relationships
Salvage Buy battlefield scrap cheap Low quality, needs refining
Commission Runners Hire people to fetch specific materials Costly but fast
PART 42: COMMANDER AI & PERSONALITY (Per Patch 7.11)
42.1 Core Rule
Every commander has:
Temperament (how they fight)
Priority (what they value)
Fear (what breaks them)
These three traits determine:
Action choice
Aid decisions
Retreat behavior
Loyalty shifts
42.2 Temperament (Roll 1d6)
Roll Temperament
1 Aggressive — Prefers Charge or Keep Attacking
2 Disciplined — Follows orders, prefers Defend
3 Cunning — Prefers Traps and terrain
4 Loyal — Mirrors allied actions
5 Proud — Never retreats first
6 Wary — Prefers Defend and Rearguard
42.3 Priority (Roll 1d6)
Roll Priority Effect
1 Glory +1 Loyalty when charging
2 Survival -1 Morale DC for own unit
3 Wealth +1 loot result
4 Victory +1d6 when Requesting Aid
5 Orders +1 Initiative if following plan
6 Banner Banner tiers up 1 battle faster
42.4 Fear (Roll 1d6)
Roll Fear Trigger
1 Fire — -1 Initiative vs fire/traps
2 Cavalry — -1d6 vs mounted units
3 Encirclement — Must test morale if flanked
4 Loss — Tests morale when ally dies
5 Dishonor — Must test morale if oath broken
6 Darkness — -1 Initiative at night
42.5 Action Selection (AI)
At start of each round, determine battlefield state:

-- 165 of 529 --

Outnumbered?
At half strength?
Terrain advantage?
Ally retreating?
Apply personality to determine action.
42.6 Aid Response Logic
When asked for Aid: Roll Loyalty Check (1d6 vs DC 4)
Condition Modifier
Same Temperament +1
Ally helped before +1
Banner Tier 3+ +1
Fear triggered -1
42.7 Personality Evolution
After each battle: Roll 1d6
Roll Change
1-2 No change
3-4 Gain new Fear
5 Temperament shifts
6 Priority shifts
42.8 Mercenary Loyalty Integration (Per Patch 7.11)
Mercenaries have additional loyalty factors:
Pay status
Contract terms
Loot share
Treatment by employer
(Use Satisfaction Score from 40.15)
42.9 Battlefield Morale for Mercenaries
Situation Morale Modifier
Pay is current +0
Pay is late -2
Pay is 1 month overdue -4 (may refuse orders)
Satisfaction 7+ +1
Satisfaction 3-4 -1
Satisfaction 1-2 -2
Fighting alongside regulars who disrespect them -1
Fighting alongside regulars who treat them as equals +1
Enemy has offered them more (they know) -3
PART 43: COMMANDER MORTALITY & ROTATION (Page 160)
Commanders track:
Age (years)
Wounds (accumulated injuries)
Loyalty (current)
Fatigue (campaign exhaustion)
Older commanders gradually retire, die, or become advisors.
PART 44: COMMANDER CAPTURE SYSTEM (Pages 161-163 + Per Patch 12.0)

-- 166 of 529 --

44.1 Step 1 — Determine How the Battle Ended
For each enemy unit that had a commander, identify its final state:
Surrendered
Routed
Destroyed
44.2 If the Enemy Surrenders
If an enemy army or unit formally surrenders:
All surviving commanders are captured
The enemy lord is captured if present
44.3 If a Unit Routes
For each routed unit's commander, resolve fate by battlefield reality:
They are captured if:
Their unit was surrounded
Their retreat path was cut
They were on foot with no escort
Friendly lines collapsed behind them
They escape if:
They had a clear retreat path
They withdrew early
They were mounted with escort
Friendly forces covered their retreat
No dice are rolled. Outcome is determined by positioning, terrain, and collapse
pattern.
44.4 If a Unit is Destroyed
When a unit is completely destroyed: Roll 1d2 for that unit's commander.
Roll Outcome
1 KILLED IN THE FIGHT — Commander dies during final moments
2 CAPTURED AMONG THE DEAD — Commander survives but is overrun
44.5 Big Battle Rule
In a major battle where the enemy line fully collapses, their banner falls, and
command structure breaks:
Assume maximum plausible capture:
Any commander who did not explicitly escape
Any noble who did not retreat earlier
...is captured.
44.6 The Knight's Value (Per Patch 12.0)
A knight is not a common soldier. He is:
A noble
Worth 50-600 SD in ransom

-- 167 of 529 --

Connected to powerful families
A potential source of intelligence
A political hostage
Default Battlefield Outcome for Knights:
80% captured (if defeated)
15% ransomed on the spot (pay and release)
5% killed (accident, feud, cruelty)
Common soldiers: Still die in droves. Knights are different.

9-10 Ransomed on field Pays 50% value immediately, released
11-12 Escaped Fled battlefield, may return later

Knight fought personally +1
Knight's side lost badly -1
Victor is known as cruel -2

Allied houses +2

Previous ransoms honored +2
Captive is heir +2 to ransom value
Captive is only heir +4 to ransom value

PART 46: BATTLEFIELD INTERVENTION SYSTEM (Pages 165-167)
46.1 Core Rule
When the player character encounters an ongoing battle, the system must ask:
Do you:
Observe fully
Position your forces
Join a side now?
46.2 Option 1 — Observe Fully
The player chooses to watch the battle resolve without interference.
Effect:
Battle runs to completion
No player input
No loot (observer rule)
Political knowledge gained
Reputation formed
Player may react after
46.3 Option 2 — Position Forces
The player does not join the battle immediately. Instead, they declare where
their units are placed around the battlefield.
Valid positions:
Enemy rear
Ally flank
High ground
Road
River crossing
Retreat path
Camp
Supply wagons
Block retreat
Intercept fleeing unit

-- 170 of 529 --

Protect wounded
Raid camp
Restrictions: Must be physically reachable. Must make sense on the map. GM can
deny impossible placement.
Battle then runs round-by-round with player allowed to each round choose ONE:
Continue waiting
Shift position
Join a side
Block retreat
Intercept fleeing unit
Protect wounded
Raid camp
46.4 Option 3 — Join a Side Now
Player picks:
Ally
Enemy
Or "I attack both"
They immediately:
Enter initiative order
Are treated as a new commander
Trigger morale effects
Trigger banner effects
Trigger reputation shifts
PART 47: PRE-BATTLE SPEECH SYSTEM (Pages 167-170)
47.1 Purpose
Ensure that before any battle begins, the system pauses to allow:
Player speech
Commander speech
War cries / morale events
47.2 Battle Start Phase
Every battle now begins with a Battle Initiation Phase before combat rolls
occur:
Army Positions Determined
PRE-BATTLE SPEECH PHASE
Morale Effects Applied

Combat Resolution
47.3 Speech Trigger Conditions
The speech phase triggers when:
Player is physically present at the battle
Player commands the army personally
Player's banner is on the field
47.4 Speech Prompt
text
Your army forms ranks across the misty field. Men check straps.
Horses stamp the ground. Banners ripple in the cold wind.
All eyes turn to you.
Your men wait for your words.
What do you say?
47.5 Speech Morale Effects
After the speech, morale bonuses are rolled. Base roll: 1d6 + Leadership
Roll Effect
1 Men uneasy (-5% morale)
2 Little effect
3 Steady morale
4 Inspired (+10% morale)
5 Battle fervor (+15% morale)
6+ Legendary (+25% morale)
47.6 The Six Blunders
Roll Blunder Effect
1 "Did He Just Say That?" Loyalty -1 after battle
2 The Wrong Enemy Rumors spread, enemy reputation boost
3 Rich Man's War Recruitment harder, peasant units lose loyalty
4 The Long One Men parody speech, commander becomes famous
5 Too Honest Morale fragile, next retreat more likely
6 Legendary Blunder Commander gains permanent flaw
PART 48: MASS COMBAT SYSTEM (Per Patch 11.0, 16.34, 16.35, 16.36)
48.1 Core Axioms
If a rule is written, it must be obeyed
If a rule is not written, it does not exist
No improvisation is permitted
All actions must resolve through defined procedures
All outcomes must be mechanical before narrative
48.2 Actions (Chosen in Secret)
Each round, every commander must choose one:
Keep Attacking (standard attack)
Defend (defensive posture)
Charge (aggressive assault)
Orderly Withdrawal (controlled retreat)
Retreat (flee the battlefield)

-- 172 of 529 --

Request Aid (call for help)
Activate Trap (only if preset)
48.3 Initiative Phase
Each commander rolls: 1d6 + Initiative Bonus
Sort from highest to lowest. Resolve actions in this order.
48.4 Action Interaction Table
Action vs Target Action Dice Modifier
Attack vs Attack +0
Attack vs Defend -1d6
Attack vs Charge +1d6
Attack vs Withdrawal +2d6
Attack vs Retreat +3d6
Charge vs Defend +1d6 to defender (defender fights back)
Charge vs Charge +1d6 to both
Charge vs Withdrawal +2d6 to charger
Charge vs Trap -2d6
Trap vs Charge +3d6
Trap vs Attack +2d6
Defend vs Attack +1d6 to defender
Defend vs Defend No attack (standoff)
Defend vs Withdrawal +2d6 to defender if attacker pursues
Defend vs Retreat +3d6 to defender if attacker pursues
48.5 Attack Dice Pool
Base Pool = Weapon Dice + (People ÷ 20)d6 + Tactics d6 + Terrain d6 + Mount
bonus (Charge only)
Apply interaction modifiers last.
NO HARD CAP — big units roll many dice.
48.6 Damage Resolution
Target unit Armor Class = X
Each d6 result:
Roll < AC = no effect
Roll ≥ AC = kill 1
Roll 6 = kill 2 (base rule)
Remove casualties immediately. If People Alive = 0 — unit defeated.
48.7 Critical Hit Rules (Per Patch 16.36)
Situation Effect
Base Rule Natural 6 = 2 kills
Target with Superb Quality Shield Natural 6 = 1 kill
Mace vs Plate Armor (AC5) Natural 6 = 3 kills
No shield / Flank / Rear attack Natural 6 = 2 kills (shield irrelevant)
Ambush round Double dice AND 6 = 2 kills
48.8 Morale
Morale Check: When unit ≤ 25% starting size: Roll 1d6
1-3: Unit must choose: Withdraw / Surrender / Attempt Parlay
4-6: Unit holds formation
Morale DC = 5 - Tier (minimum DC 2)
Half Strength Penalty (Per Patch 21.0):
When unit falls to 50% or less, lose HALF of commander's morale bonus (rounded

-- 173 of 529 --

down):
Commander Tier Full Bonus Half Bonus (≤50%)
Tier 1 +1 +0
Tier 2 +2 +1
Tier 3 +3 +1
Tier 4 +4 +2
Tier 5 +5 +2
48.9 Request Aid
A unit may spend its action to call for help.
Request Aid:
Choose 1 friendly unit not Engaged
That unit may immediately attack the enemy you are Engaged with
The aiding unit becomes Engaged with that enemy
You remain Engaged
48.10 Commander Reaction on Death
Roll 1d6 when commander dies:
Roll Result
1 Unit Shatters — disbands immediately
2-3 Unit Wavers — loses 1 Tier, immediate Morale Check
4-5 Unit Holds — -1 Initiative for rest of battle
6 Unit Rallies — fights on normally, may promote from within
48.11 Victory Conditions
Battle ends when:
All enemy units are defeated
OR all enemy units retreat
If all retreat: Begin Chase System.
PART 49: ENGAGEMENT LOCK SYSTEM (Pages 184-186)
49.1 Engaging a Unit
When a unit successfully attacks or is attacked by another unit, those two units
are now Engaged.
49.2 Effect of Engagement
While Engaged:
A unit cannot change targets
A unit cannot Charge a different unit
A unit cannot Withdraw without penalty
A unit may only attack the unit it is Engaged with
49.3 Breaking Engagement
A unit may stop being Engaged only if:
One unit is destroyed, routed, or withdraws
OR Request Aid is used
49.4 Multiple Units on One Target
If two friendly units are Engaged with one enemy unit:

-- 174 of 529 --

Both may attack it normally
Enemy still only attacks one unit per turn
49.5 Multi-Unit Engagement
A single unit may be engaged by up to 3 enemy units simultaneously.
When a unit is engaged by multiple enemies:
Enemies Engaged Penalty
2 units -1 Armor Class
3 units -2 Armor Class (maximum)
49.6 Breakout Attempt
Instead of attacking, defender may attempt to disengage:
Roll 1d6 + Commander Initiative vs DC 5 + number of engaged enemies
Result Outcome
Success Unit breaks engagement and may move 1 zone
Failure Unit remains engaged and suffers +1 casualties this round
PART 50: PLAYER COMMAND AUTHORITY (Pages 186-188)
50.1 Player Control Limit
The player only gives orders to their own unit. The player may NOT:
Direct allied units
Choose allied targets
Choose allied actions
Override allied commanders
50.2 Allied Unit Behavior
Each allied unit:
Chooses its own target
Chooses its own action
May use Request Aid
May ignore the player
50.3 The Return Protocol
When ANY player-controlled unit completes its assigned task and the player is
NOT present with them, the following happens automatically:
Step 1 — Mission Complete: Unit achieves objective.
Step 2 — Return Destination: Unit automatically begins return travel to:
IF LANDED: The player's PRIMARY CAPITAL HOLDING
IF LANDLESS: The player's CURRENT LOCATION
Step 3 — Travel & Tracking:
Calculate travel time normally
Unit consumes supply during journey
Unit may be intercepted, ambushed, or delayed
Exceptions: Unit does NOT automatically return if:

-- 175 of 529 --

Explicit orders to stay
Pursuit orders
Unit destroyed
Unit defects
PART 51: COORDINATED WITHDRAWAL (Pages 188-189)
51.1 Trigger
If all living allied commanders choose Withdrawal in the same round:
They activate Coordinated Withdrawal.
51.2 Effect
All withdrawing units gain: +1 Armor Class for:
The next round of combat
OR the first round of a chase scene
51.3 Options Created
When Coordinated Withdrawal triggers, the withdrawing side may choose:
Option Effect
Coordinated Retreat Units attempt to leave the battlefield together
Defensive Line Units gain +1 AC and fight one more round
Parley Attempt If enemy is not overwhelmingly superior, commanders may
attempt negotiation
Surrender Ends battle immediately
PART 52: CHASE SYSTEM (Pages 189-191)
52.1 When a Chase Begins
A Chase begins if:
Any enemy unit Retreats
AND at least one opposing commander chooses Keep Attacking or Charge
52.2 No Chase
If all enemy units Retreat and no one pursues: Battle ends immediately.
52.3 Chase Round Structure
Each Chase resolves in Rounds. Each round:
Retreating side chooses: Flee (speed) / Rearguard (delay) / Scatter (evasion)
Pursuing side chooses: Pursue / Encircle / Break Off
Actions chosen secretly.
52.4 Chase Roll
Each side rolls: 1d6 + Initiative Bonus + Banner Bonus
Condition Modifier
Mounted +1
Light armor +1
Heavy armor -1
Coordinated Withdrawal bonus active +1
Terrain favors escape +1
Terrain favors pursuit +1
52.5 Result
Result Outcome
Retreating side > Pursuing side Escape
Equal Clash (1 attack roll only)

-- 176 of 529 --

Pursuing side > Retreating side Caught
52.6 Retreat Action Effects
Action Effect
Flee +1 to Chase roll, -1 AC this round
Rearguard Enemy attack dice -1d6, Morale check required
Scatter +2 to Chase roll, lose 1d6 people automatically
PART 53: AMBUSH SYSTEM (Pages 191-193 + Per Patch 16.36)
53.1 Core Principle
If they don't see you, they don't get a turn.
53.2 When an Ambush May Occur
An ambush may only occur when:
One force is concealed
The other force is unaware
Terrain allows hiding
The ambushing side chooses to strike
53.3 Ambush Check
1d6 + modifiers:
Condition Modifier
Forest +2
Hills +1
Night +2
Snow or fog +1
From high ground +1
Enemy is marching +1
Enemy is hungry/tired +1
Scouts +1
Fortified road/bridge +1
Alert posture +1
Recently warned +1
Local guides +1
53.4 Result
Roll Outcome
< DC No ambush, normal battle
≥ DC but < DC+3 Partial Success — Ambusher chooses: strike OR withdraw
≥ DC+3 Full Success — Ambush Round triggers
53.5 The Ambush Round
If Full Success: This becomes Round 0 of the battle.
During this round:
Ambushing force issues full orders
Ambushed force takes NO ACTIONS
Ambushing force rolls DOUBLE ATTACK DICE
No morale checks yet
No retreats
No request aid
No late joins
No reactions of any kind
This round is PURE SLAUGHTER.

-- 177 of 529 --

Then: Battle begins normally at Round 1.
53.6 Example — Ambush Round
300 archers normally: 3d6 + 15d6 = 18d6
Ambush double: 36d6
Devastating casualties in first moments
Battle often decided before it begins
PART 54: SIEGE & FORTIFICATIONS (Per Patch 18.1-18.6, 19.0, 20.0, 20.1)
54.1 Core Principle
Fortifications change everything. A wall is not just an AC bonus — it is a force
multiplier, a morale anchor, and a bloody problem for anyone who wants what's
behind it.
54.2 Fortification Types (Per Patch 18.1)
Fortification Ranged Rerolls First Melee Bonus Breach Effect
Wooden Palisade 1 per unit/round +0 Destroyed when scaled
Stone Wall 2 per unit/round +1 Bonus lost when breached
Fortress 3 per unit/round +2 Bonus lost when breached
City Walls 2 per unit/round +1 Bonus lost when breached
Reroll Mechanic: Reroll up to limit of dice that would have hit.
First Melee Bonus: Extra +1/+2 on round of first contact.
54.3 Wall Fighting Combat Density (Per Patch 18.2)
Defender Fighting Capacity: ALL defenders on that wall section fight at once.
Unlimited.
Attacker Fighting Capacity: Limited by assault rate.
54.4 Assault Action
Assault replaces Charge when attacking fortifications.
Effect:
Attacker rolls normal attack dice
Defender AC includes fortification bonus
Attacker casualties are doubled on failed attack
54.5 Siege Preparation Phase
Before a siege begins, attacker may choose ONE:
Option Effect
Build ramps Ignore +1 AC
Dig trenches Defender loses initiative bonus
Starve out No combat for 3 turns, defender morale check
Bombard Make 1 attack roll per turn with half dice
54.6 Breaching
If an Assault roll deals 10+ kills in one round: Wall is breached
54.7 Siege Ladder Integration (Per Patch 18.3)
Rule Detail
Carrying 4 men per ladder, FULL AC while moving
Climbing Rate 10 men per ladder per round
Climbing AC AC 2 while climbing (no shield, one hand occupied)
Ladder Destruction (Fire arrows) 6 = ignite
Ladder Destruction (Pushing) 4+ = ladder pushed, 1d6 climbers die
Ladder Capacity Bonus Every 4 ladders = +40 men/round to section
54.8 Approach Times (Per Patch 18.5)
Asset Rounds to Wall Men/Round
LADDERS 2 10 per ladder

-- 178 of 529 --

Siege Towers 4 20 per tower
Battering Ram 5 Targets gate
Ladders arrive FIRST, forcing defenders to split attention before towers arrive.
54.9 Defender's Fury (Per Patch 18.6)
Ranged Units on Walls — DOUBLE DICE:
Formula = 2 × [Weapon Dice + (Troops ÷ 20)d6]
100 Archers: 16d6 instead of 8d6
Melee on Walls — PERMANENT +1 TO ALL ATTACKS:
Any defender on own walls gets +1 to ALL melee attack rolls for ENTIRE battle.
Kill Ratio: Defenders kill ~4 attackers per defender lost.
Attackers need 6:1 numerical advantage to overwhelm.
54.10 The Moment of Impact (Per Patch 18.4)
Round of arrival:
Wall rerolls LOST for that section
First wave climbs (ladders: 10 each, towers: 20 each)
Combat begins immediately
Defenders get FIRST MELEE BONUS
Sustained Assault — Each Subsequent Round:
New attackers climb (ladders: 10 each, towers: 20 each)
ALL attackers on wall (new + survivors) fight
ALL defenders on wall fight (with PERMANENT +1 to all attacks)
Casualties removed
54.11 Siege Events (Per Patch 4.1)
Attacker Siege Events (1d6 per week):
Roll Event Resolution
1 Disease in Camp 1d6 × 10 soldiers sick. Roll Morale for all units. On
failure, -1 Morale.
2 Supply Raid Defenders sortie at night. Lose 1d6 × 5 FSU if no guards.
3 Desertion 1d6 × 5 soldiers leave. Roll Loyalty for each commander. On 1-
2, they lose 1 Loyalty.
4 Engine Trouble One siege engine (random) needs repair. Takes 3 days,
cannot fire.
5 Rumor Reaches Camp Roll 1d6 + Recon: 1-3 = false rumor (morale -1),
4-6 = true info (+1 to next assault)
6 Relief Force Rumored Roll 1d6: 1-3 = false alarm, 4-6 = relief actually
coming in 1d6 weeks
Defender Siege Events (1d6 per week):
Roll Event Resolution
1 Disease Inside Walls 1d6 × 5 civilians sick. Roll Morale for garrison.
On failure, -1 Morale.
2 Food Shortage Lose 1d6 × 2 FSU (spoilage, theft, rats). If below
starvation threshold, begin Part 60.
3 Morale Failure Roll 1d6 for each commander (Fear triggers). On 1, they
want to surrender.
4 Wall Damage One section weakened. Next assault gets +1d6 to breach roll.
5 Messenger Attempt Roll 1d6: 1-3 = caught (no help), 4-6 = gets through
(relief may come)

-- 179 of 529 --

6 Sortie Success Attacker loses 1d6 × 5 soldiers and 1d3 × 10 FSU.
Defender gains +1 Morale.
54.12 Siege Morale Track
Track siege morale separately for attacker and defender:
Week Attacker Morale Mod Defender Morale Mod
Week 1 0 0
Week 2 -1 (homesickness) 0
Week 3 -1 (boredom) -1 (confinement)
Week 4 -2 (winter coming) -2 (hope fading)
Week 5+ -3 (why are we here?) -3 (we will die here)
Modifiers:
Each failed Morale event: -1 cumulative
Each successful sortie: +1 defender
Each supply raid: -1 attacker
Food low: -1 per week below half rations
When Morale reaches -5: Automatic surrender attempt.
54.13 Defender Morale Checks
Defenders must test morale when:
Trigger DC
Wall breached (first time) 5
25% of wall lost 4
50% of wall lost 3
Gate destroyed 4
Commander killed on walls 5
Supply wagon destroyed 4
Unit hits half strength 4
Banner captured 5
54.14 Attacker Morale Checks
Attackers must test morale when:
Trigger DC
Tower destroyed 4
Ladder destroyed 3
25% casualties in assault 4
Morale Check Formula: 1d6 + Commander Tier
Result < DC = Break (retreat, surrender, rout)
54.15 Sapping & Mining (Per Patch 4.1)
If attackers have engineers, they may attempt to mine under the walls.
Requirements: 2+ weeks, engineers alive, no counter-mines
Resolution: Roll 1d6 at end of Week 2, Week 4, etc.:
Roll Result
1 Tunnel collapses, 1d6 engineers die
2-3 Progress slow (try again next week)
4-5 Tunnel complete under wall
6 Tunnel complete + defenders unaware
If tunnel complete: Next assault ignores +2 AC from walls.
Counter-mining: Defenders with engineers may roll 1d6 each week. On 6, they find
and collapse attacker tunnel.
54.16 Siege Tower Targeting & Tar Defense (Per Patch 20.1)
Rule Detail

Burning Tower Effects:
Round Effect

54.17 Night Assault (Per Patch 20.0)

New moon -3

54.18 Surrender Terms (Per Patch 4.1)

2d6 Result Terms
2-4 Massacre City sacked, all killed. Attacker +2 Fear, -3 Honor
5-7 Harsh Leaders executed, soldiers enslaved, city looted
8-10 Standard Ransom paid, garrison free, city intact
11-12 Honorable Free withdrawal with arms and banners
13+ Merciful Defender keeps city as vassal, no punishment

56.7 Refugee Creation from Raids (Per Patch 9.10)
For every 10 population killed or captured, 1d6 refugees flee to nearby
holdings.

Refugees arrive in 1d3 days.

+X to Labor Pool
+X to Recruitment Pool
-1 Local Morale for 1 month (strain on resources)
+1 FSU consumption per 100 refugees
56.8 Post-Raid Holding Status (Per Patch 9.10)
After a raid, the holding's stats update:
Stat Update
Population Reduced by (Killed + Captured + Fled)
Labor Pool Recalculate at 40% of new population
Garrison Unchanged (unless targeted)
Income -10% for 1d6 months (disruption)
Loyalty -1 to -3 depending on severity
Opinion of raider -2 to -4 permanent

Target is not expecting waterborne attack

PART 57: LOOT SYSTEM (Per Patch 7.28)
57.1 Core Rule
After every successful battle, generate loot based on enemy equipment and battle
outcome.
57.2 Loot Generation by Battle Size
SKIRMISH (1-49 combatants per side) — Roll 1d6
Roll Result
1-2 Salvage only (weapon/armor cost ÷ 4 in SD)
3-4 Partial equipment (cost ÷ 2 in SD + 1d3 random goods)
5-6 Full equipment (full cost in SD + 1d6 random goods)
FIELD BATTLE (50-200 combatants per side) — Roll 2d6
Roll Result
2-4 Salvage only + 1d6 × 5 SD
5-7 Partial equipment + 2d6 × 5 SD
8-10 Full equipment + 3d6 × 5 SD
11-12 Rich spoils + 4d6 × 5 SD + 1 rare item
MAJOR BATTLE (200+ combatants per side) — Roll 3d6
Roll Result
3-6 Partial equipment + 3d6 × 10 SD
7-12 Full equipment + 4d6 × 10 SD
13-15 Rich spoils + 5d6 × 10 SD + 1d3 rare items
16-18 Legendary loot + 6d6 × 10 SD + 1d6 rare items + possible artifact
57.3 Salvage Only
Gain: (weapon cost + Armor cost) ÷ 4 in Silverdew, rounded down.
Example: Chainmail (25) + Spear (12) = 37 ÷ 4 = 9 SD
57.4 Partial Equipment
Gain: (weapon cost + Armor cost) ÷ 2 in Silverdew, rounded down.
AND Roll 1d6 on Salvage Finds table.
57.5 Full Equipment
Gain: (weapon cost + Armor cost) in Silverdew.
AND Recover one weapon and one armor set (may be equipped, sold, or stored).
57.6 Rich Spoils

-- 186 of 529 --

Gain: (weapon cost + Armor cost) × 2 in Silverdew.
AND Recover 1d3 weapons and 1d3 armor sets.
AND Roll once on Rare Items table.
57.7 Salvage Finds Table (1d6)
Roll Result
1 1d6 trade goods (random)
2 1d3 bundles of furs (value 10 SD each)
3 1d6 units of timber or iron
4 A fine weapon (worth 2× normal cost) — includes pikes, javelins
5 A serviceable armor set (sell for 75% cost) — includes shields
6 A commander's personal item (value 2d6 × 10 SD)

57.16 Observer No-Loot Rule
If the player character:
Did not command a unit
Did not lead a banner
Did not participate in the battle
Then:
They receive no loot
They make no loot decisions
They cannot claim gear from the field
They cannot profit from NPC vs NPC battles
Loot belongs only to:
Victors who fought
Units that survived
Lords who held ground
PART 58: VISIBLE LOOT OUTPUT RULE (Pages 222-223)
58.1 Trigger
This rule triggers immediately after:
Battle
Camp capture
Supply wagon capture
Siege victory
It triggers before:
Recruitment
Replacement
Tier checks
58.2 Mandatory Narrative Loot Summary
After loot is mechanically generated, the system must output a visible in-world
summary using this structure:
LOOT SUMMARY FORMAT:
What was taken
How it looks
What it means
Example:
text

-- 189 of 529 --

Your men strip the fallen of usable blades and bent mail.

58.3 Mechanical Silence Clause
The following must NOT be shown:
Dice results
Table names
Silverdew values
Equipment costs
Roll categories
Unless the player explicitly says: "Show me the loot rolls."
58.4 Player Override
If the player requests transparency:
Format:
text
LOOT REVEAL:
Source: [source]
Roll: [result]
Value: [amount]
After reveal, narration resumes normally.
