C.2 GLOSSARY OF TERMS
A
AC (Armor Class) — Target number from 2-5; attack rolls must meet or exceed this
to score a kill. Determined by armor type and shields.

-- 334 of 529 --

After-Action Report — Post-battle summary showing casualties, loot, and
consequences. Generated after every significant engagement.
Ambush Round — Free round of combat for concealed attackers; ambushed force
cannot act. Ambushers roll double dice.
Armor Class — See AC.
Artificer — Landless profession focused on crafting weapons, armor, and tools.
Skill levels 0-5 determine crafting ability.
Assassin — Landless profession focused on killing for coin. Uses Part 95
mechanics.
Axiom of Completeness — Core rule stating: If it is not written, it does not
exist. If it is written, it must be obeyed.
B
Back Room Deal — Hidden agreement between 2+ houses about a future action. May
be discovered through espionage.
Baker — Landless profession focused on baking bread and other goods. Uses Part
74 conversion rates.
Ballista — Tier I siege engine. Anti-personnel, 4 crew, 40 SD cost.
Banner — Symbol of a unit; provides special bonuses; tiers up with battle wins.
Tiers 1-5.
Banneret — Knight commander, Tier 2-3 noble. Commands 20-49 soldiers.
Bastion — Upgraded castle (Tier 5) with stronger defenses and fear effect.
Income 1,500 SD/month.
Battering Ram — Tier II siege engine. Gate destruction, 25 crew, 180 SD cost.
Battle Horn — Horn type used by lords and commanders. Deep, urgent sound.
Bending — Formal act of swearing fealty to a regional king. Creates vassal
relationship.
Blackmail — Using discovered secrets to force compliance. See Part 92.7-92.9.
Black Market — Illegal trading network. Access DC varies by settlement. Prices
0.5x-1.8x.
Blood Feud — Multi-generational conflict from murder or betrayal. Opinion -3
permanent.
Blood Purity — Measure of how closely related a claimant is to previous kings.
Used in Southern Mountains succession.
Bombard — Tier III siege engine. Early cannon, devastating area damage, 500 SD
cost.
Branch Family — Cadet branch of a main noble house. Controls 1-3 resource
patches, pays tribute.
Breach — Wall breach in siege. Occurs when assault deals 10+ kills in one round.
Bridge Rule — Each bridge is a strategic target, fortified, political, tracked
as minor holding.

-- 335 of 529 --

Bulk Purchase — Buying food in quantity. Tiers: Spot (0%), Bulk (-10%),
Wholesale (-20%), Contract (-30%), Strategic (-40%).
C
Camp Phase — Post-battle period where commanders may recruit, train, fortify,
scout, or drill.
Cancel Protocol — Player may type "CANCEL" at any time to abort current
operation and return to System Launcher.
Canonical World Memory — All locations, characters, factions, wars, and events
become permanent. Cannot be contradicted.
Capital — Tier 8 holding. Population 150,000+, income 15,000 SD/month.
Caravan — Trade convoy. Tiers I-V with increasing capacity and profit bonuses.
Castle — Tier 4 fortification. Income 900 SD/month, AC +2, ranged rerolls
2/round.
Catapult — Tier I siege engine. Anti-infantry, minor wall damage, 60 SD cost.
Champion's Duel — Single combat between two chosen warriors to decide battle
outcome without mass bloodshed.
Charge — Combat action. Aggressive assault, bonuses per Action Interaction
Table.
Chase — Pursuit after retreat. Resolved in rounds with Flee/Rearguard/Scatter vs
Pursue/Encircle/Break Off.
Citadel — Tier 7 fortification. Income 3,750 SD/month, AC +3, ranged rerolls
3/round.
City — Tier 5 holding. Population 15,000-30,000, income 2,625 SD/month.
Civil War — Succession conflict. Phases 1-4, from Disputed Succession to
Collapse.
Claim — Legal right to a title or land. Can be pressed in war. Strength:
Strong/Moderate/Weak.
Clan Name — Among nomads, the blood name spoken only among kin. Never spoken to
outsiders.
Closed System Mode — No new resources, no inferred resources, no assumed
medieval materials. If not in system, it doesn't exist.
Coin Prince — Wealthy mercenary contractor. Tracked in G.19.
Commander — Leader of a unit. Has tier, AC, weapon, loyalty, temperament,
priority, fear.
Command Capacity — Number of soldiers a commander can effectively control. Base
× Banner multiplier.
Common Quality — Tier 1 quality. Standard stats, no bonuses.
Consumption — Phase 2 of Weekly Economic Cycle. Garrisons, armies, and
population consume food.
Contingency — Schemer's backup plans that trigger if discovered.
Coordinated Withdrawal — Organized retreat when all commanders choose

-- 336 of 529 --

withdrawal. +1 AC.
Courtship — Romance actions: Gift, Flattery, Shared time, Protection, Song,
Letters, Rescue, Political support, Secret meetings.
Crafting — Converting raw materials into finished goods. Requires facilities,
labor, time.
Critical Hit — Natural 6 on attack die. Base = 2 kills. Modified by shields,
weapons, situation.
Crossbow — Weapon with Armor Piercing special: Hit AC 5 on 4+, AC 6 on 5+.
Crownbound Church — One of Three Great Faiths. Supports heirs, condemns
oathbreakers.
Cult — Secret religious organization. Can be founded for 120 SD, 20 timber, 4
weeks.
Currency — Silverdew (SD) only. 10-20 SD arms a small unit.
D
Dagger — Fastest weapon (2d6, Init +2). Lowest damage.
Daily Cycle — Weather, local events, player actions, time advances.
Dark Season Names — Regional names for seasons: Mudbirth, Firetime, Redfall,
Hungerwake, etc.
Dead King Axiom — At campaign start, no true king lives. Throne empty,
legitimacy shattered.
Deepfrost — Winter month. Harshest winter conditions.
Defend — Combat action. Defensive posture, fights back when attacked.
Defender's Fury — Siege rule: Ranged units on walls roll double dice, melee
gains permanent +1.
Desertion — Soldiers abandoning unit due to low morale or starvation. Roll 1d6
weekly.
Destrier — Heavy warhorse. Cost 220 SD, +1 Init, reroll 3 missed dice, +3d6 on
Charge.
Detection — Night raid mechanic. Influenced by scouts, moon, alertness.
Directional Navigation — Describing location by direction and days only. No
coordinates.
Disease — Infection risk from unsanitary conditions. Progression Mild → Moderate
→ Severe → Mortal.
Dowry — Payment at marriage. Types: Coin, Land, Political.
Duel — Single combat between commanders. Uses Part 62 mechanics.
Duelist Reputation — Titles from Untested to The Unmatched. Grants bonuses to
duels.
Dynasty — Line of rulers. Continues as long as any blood relative exists.
E
Economic Cycle — Weekly: Production → Consumption → Trade & Tribute → Wages &

-- 337 of 529 --

Upkeep → Treasury Update → Time Confirmation.
Eldric the Black Drake — Legendary commander NPC. Tier 4 commander, Tier 5 war
marshal.
Empty Throne — The throne of the old king, empty for 30 years. Eight crowns
scattered.
Engagement — Units locked in combat; cannot change targets until one side breaks
or Request Aid is used.
Engine Restrictions — Never improve missing systems, never simulate unwritten
systems, never create "simple versions."
Engineer — Specialist unit for building and operating siege engines. 10
engineers, 25 SD cost.
Epithet — Earned name reflecting deeds: "the Bold," "the Cruel," etc.
Escape — Prisoner escape attempts. Difficulty varies by restraints.
Espionage — Spy operations to discover secrets. Roll 1d6 + skill.
Event Capacity — Limit on major events per region to prevent chaos. Small:1,
Normal:2, Major:3.
Event Escalation — Gradual development: Rumor → small incidents → local
disturbances → organized activity → open conflict.
Event Weight System — Tracks major vs minor events per region.
Excommunication — Loss of faith standing. Cannot receive blessings, -2 Opinion
with co-religionists.
Exiled Noble — Landless profession. Fallen lord with loyal followers.
Extreme Weather — The Long Night, White Hurricane, Great Flood, Fire Autumn, The
Dry, The Still Wind.
F
Faction — Noble house or political entity with goals and resources.
Failed Action Rule — If action requires system not in document, action
automatically fails narratively.
Faith Influence — Measure of a house's standing with religion (0-6). Affects
blessings, excommunication.
False Surrender — Defender surrenders falsely to gain surprise. Grants
Oathbreaker trait.
Family Council — Tribal decision-making body. Families have voice in chief's
decisions.
Family Reputation — Tribal family standing: Selfish, Struggling, Neutral,
Generous, Honored, Legendary.
Farmer — Landless profession. Works the land, grows food, raises stock.
Fear — Commander trait. What breaks them: Fire, Cavalry, Encirclement, Loss,
Dishonor, Darkness.
Feast Tiers — Subsistence (10 SD), Decent (25 SD), Comfortable (50 SD), Generous
(100 SD), Opulent (200+ SD).

-- 338 of 529 --

Fealty — Oath of loyalty from vassal to liege. Creates feudal bond.
Festival — Celebration attracting pilgrims and merchants. Costs 50-2,000 SD.
Feudal Web — Hierarchy of lords, vassals, and knights. 16 Great Lords, 107 Minor
Lords, 340 Landed Knights.
Field Army — Mobile forces player commands. Costs treasury to maintain.
Fifty, The — 50 elite operatives with attrition. Used for raids, sabotage,
assassination.
Fisher — Landless profession. Lives from the water, follows the fish.
Flavor Origin — Permanent true detail about character's land or band. Creates
story events.
Flooding the Market — Dumping too much of a good crashes prices. Roll 1d6 +
Flood Ratio.
Food Goods — Edible resources convertible to FSU: Grain, Fish, Salted Meat,
Dried Fruit, Cheese, Livestock.
Food Supply Unit (FSU) — 1 FSU feeds 100 soldiers for one week. 100 SU food = 1
FSU.
Foraging — Living off the land. 1d6 per week, yields 0.25-1 FSU depending on
terrain.
Fortification — Defensive structure adding AC, ranged rerolls, and melee bonuses
to garrison.
Fortified Manor — Tier 2 fortification. Income 225 SD/month.
Fortress — Tier 6 fortification. Income 2,250 SD/month, AC +3, ranged rerolls
3/round.
Free Men — Population available for recruitment. Part of Labor Pool.
Frostwane — Winter month. Beginning of winter.
FSU — See Food Supply Unit.
G
Garrison — Forces that stay home, defend holding automatically. Free (built into
holding income).
Gathering — Collecting herbs, wood, etc. 1d6 per week, DC varies by terrain.
Gender Norms — Structural realities modeled by existing systems (inheritance,
marriage, loyalty, reputation).
General — Commander of one army in a Grand Army. Reports to War Marshal.
Given Name — Personal name. Generated from regional tables.
Goods — Resources tracked in Supply Units (SU). Food, Trade, Strategic
categories.
Grace Period — First 3 years of campaign: no random rare events trigger.
Grand Army — Multiple armies united under a War Marshal. Tiers: Small (2-3
armies) to Epic (11+).

-- 339 of 529 --

Grand Ball — Political event. Costs 150 SD, 200 FSU, 50 trade goods.
Grand Melee — Tournament event. Mass combat with blunted weapons.
Great Lord — Tier 5-6 noble. Commands 3,000 personal soldiers, 8,000+ with
vassals.
Great Trebuchet — Tier III siege engine. Catastrophic wall damage, 350 SD cost.
Greening — Spring month. Planting season.
Grudge — Public or secret hostility between houses. Affects Opinion and
interactions.
Guild — Merchant or craft organization. Membership levels: Apprentice,
Journeyman, Master, Guildmaster.
H
Half Strength Penalty — When unit ≤50%, lose half of commander's morale bonus.
Halberd — Weapon. 4d6, Init -1, +2d6 vs mounted, lowers mounted AC by -1.
Hamlet — Tier 0 holding. Population 100-200, income 75 SD/month.

END OF APPENDIX C — REFERENCE & INDEX
APPENDIX D: DESIGN PHILOSOPHY (Pages 411-416)
D.1 THE RULES OF CERTAINTY

-- 350 of 529 --

The entire system rests on a single foundation: certainty. Every rule is
written. Every outcome is determined. No ambiguity. No interpretation. No "GM
fiat."
This is not because the designer is controlling. It is because the player
deserves to know that the world operates by consistent laws. When a player makes
a choice, they should be able to predict — within reason — what will happen. Not
because they know the dice, but because they understand the world.
The Rules of Certainty (Part 1) are not suggestions. They are the bedrock. If a
rule exists, it must be obeyed. If a rule does not exist, it cannot be used.
This creates a bounded, finite world — one that can be learned, mastered, and
predicted.
D.2 THE BRUTALIST DESIGN PHILOSOPHY
This system is brutalist. Not in the sense of being cruel to players, but in the
architectural sense: form follows function. Everything exists because it must.
Nothing exists for "flavor" alone — if it's in the rules, it matters.
The world is not designed to be fair. It is designed to be real. Lords die.
Armies starve. Lines of succession fail. The strong do not always win —
sometimes the clever do, sometimes the lucky do, sometimes the stubborn do.
But the rules themselves are fair. They apply equally to everyone. The same
mechanics that let a player raise an army also let NPC lords raise theirs. The
same supply rules that constrain the player also constrain their enemies. The
same succession crises that threaten the player can topple their rivals.
This is not a game about "winning." It is a game about experiencing a world.
D.3 THE PATCH ARCHITECTURE
The system is designed to be patched. Not because it's broken, but because it's
alive. As players discover edge cases, as new ideas emerge, as the world expands
— patches are the mechanism for growth.
Every patch follows these principles:
Authority: Only Rule 1.4 can authorize a patch
Integration: Patches are not separate documents — they are integrated into the
main text
Versioning: Every patch increments the version number
Transparency: All patches are documented in the patch log
Necessity: Patches fix gaps or add depth — never for "cool factor" alone
The document you are reading now has integrated over 50 patches into a single,
unified text. No patch archaeology required. The current version is all that
matters.
D.4 SCARCITY AS STORYTELLING
This world is scarce. Not because the designer is cruel, but because scarcity
creates story.
Food is scarce — so every meal matters, every harvest is tense, every siege is
desperate
Equipment is scarce — so every sword has a history, every suit of plate is an
investment
Coin is scarce — so every silverdew has weight, every payment is felt

-- 351 of 529 --

Population is scarce — so every soldier counts, every death is mourned
Time is scarce — so every season matters, every campaign must be planned
In a world of abundance, nothing matters. In a world of scarcity, everything
matters.
The player feels this scarcity not as frustration, but as meaning. When you
finally afford that castle, when your army survives winter, when your heir is
born healthy — those moments matter because they were hard-won.
D.5 THE PLAYER AS COMPONENT, NOT THE CENTER
The world does not revolve around the player. It never has. It never will.
Armies march whether the player watches or not. Lords scheme whether the player
interferes or not. Children are born, people die, seasons turn — all without the
player's input.
The player is not the hero of the world. They are a component within it —
sometimes influential, sometimes irrelevant, sometimes merely another face in
the crowd.
This is not cruelty. It is liberation. The player is free to pursue their own
goals without the weight of "saving the world." They can be ambitious or humble,
warlike or peaceful, political or reclusive. The world will react accordingly —
but it will not wait for them.
D.6 THE BEAUTY OF DETERMINISM
This system uses dice, but you will never see them. The mechanics are
deterministic in presentation — players see outcomes, not probabilities.
This is intentional. In the real world, you do not know the odds. You do not see
the dice. You only see what happens.
When you negotiate with a lord, you do not know if he will agree. You only know
what he says. When you march to battle, you do not know if you will win. You
only know the result.
This determinism creates tension, immersion, and consequence. Every choice
matters because you cannot see the math behind it. You must act on information,
not probabilities.
D.7 THE SILENCE OF THE MECHANICS
The mechanics are silent. They exist, but they are not seen. Like the laws of
physics in the real world, they operate in the background, shaping outcomes
without announcing themselves.
This silence serves three purposes:
Immersion: Players experience the world, not the rules
Tension: Not knowing the odds creates genuine uncertainty
Trust: Players trust that the system is fair because they cannot manipulate it
When a player succeeds, they feel they earned it. When they fail, they feel it
was deserved. The silence ensures that credit and blame go to the choices, not
the dice.
D.8 THE LIVING WORLD AS ANTAGONIST
In many games, the antagonist is a specific villain — the Dark Lord, the Evil
Empire, the Big Bad. In this world, the antagonist is the world itself.
The cold is an antagonist. Hunger is an antagonist. Time is an antagonist.

-- 352 of 529 --

Distance is an antagonist. The ambitions of other lords are antagonists. The
weight of history is an antagonist.
The player does not fight against a single enemy. They fight against entropy,
against scarcity, against the grinding reality of a world that does not care
about them.
This is not nihilism. It is realism. And when the player overcomes these
obstacles — when they survive the winter, when they feed their army, when they
outlast their enemies — the victory is genuine.
D.9 THE OATH AS CORE METAPHOR
The game is called Age of Shattered Oaths for a reason. Oaths are the glue that
holds society together. They are also the first thing to break.
A lord swears fealty to a king — then betrays him.
A knight swears to protect the weak — then preys on them.
A husband swears to love his wife — then takes a lover.
A father swears to provide for his children — then drinks away the grain money.
Every oath broken creates a crack in the world. Enough cracks, and the whole
thing shatters.
The player will make oaths. They will break them, or they will keep them. Either
way, the world will remember. The ledger will record. The ancestors will watch.
An oath kept is a small victory against entropy.
An oath broken is another crack in the world.
D.10 THE DESIGNER'S RESPONSIBILITY
A system this detailed carries responsibility. Every rule affects how players
experience the world. Every table shapes what is possible. Every number
determines what matters.
The designer's responsibility is to:
Be clear: Rules must be unambiguous
Be complete: No missing steps, no gaps to fill
Be consistent: No contradictions, no surprises
Be fair: The same rules apply to everyone
Be silent: Let the mechanics fade into the background
The goal is not to create a game that is "fun" in the traditional sense. The
goal is to create a world that is real. Fun will emerge from that reality — the
fun of survival, of achievement, of story.
D.11 FINAL WORD
This is the Age of Shattered Oaths. The old king is dead. The eight crowns are
scattered. The throne sits empty.
You are not the chosen one. You are not the hero of prophecy. You are a person
in a world of people, trying to make your way through the chaos.
You will swear oaths. You may break them. You will make allies. You may lose
them. You will build. You may see it all burn.
But the ledger will remember. The world will remember. And when you finally lay
down your banner, your story will be recorded in the Hall of Legends.
Ride wisely. Write your name in the chronicles of this broken world.

-- 353 of 529 --

END OF APPENDIX D — DESIGN PHILOSOPHY
APPENDIX E: REGIONAL CUISINE (Pages 705-708)
E.1 CORE PRINCIPLE
A lord's table is a display of his land's wealth. What is served, how it is
served, who sits where — all of it speaks. Empty plates speak loudest of all.
E.2 FEAST TIERS
Tier Cost (SD) Food Required Description
1 — Subsistence 10 SD 2 FSU Simple fare, barely enough, guests notice. -1
Opinion.
2 — Decent 25 SD 4 FSU Adequate portions, nothing fancy, no shame. No modifier.
3 — Comfortable 50 SD 8 FSU Good bread, meat regularly, ale flows. +1 Opinion.
4 — Generous 100 SD 15 FSU Multiple courses, imported goods,
spices appear. +2 Opinion, +1 Reputation.
5 — Opulent 200+ SD 25+ FSU Exotic dishes, rare delicacies, table
groans. +3 Opinion, +2 Reputation, stories told for years.
E.3 CENTRAL PLAINS CUISINE
Wheat and barley breads, roasted meats (beef, pork, mutton), hearty stews with
root vegetables, ale and small beer. Lords add spices (imported, expensive).
Cheese from sheep and cattle. Apples, pears in season.
Lord's Table: Multiple meats, white bread (not brown), imported wine, spiced
dishes. Shows agricultural wealth.
Commoner's Table: Brown bread, pottage (grain and vegetable stew), small beer,
cheese. Meat on feast days.
Festival Foods: Roasted whole ox (at grand feasts), honeyed apples, spiced ale.
Regional Specialties:
Iron Hills lamb — raised on mineral-rich grass, distinct flavor
River Caedor trout — fresh, grilled with herbs
Plains cheese — aged, hard, travels well
E.4 WESTERN RIVERS CUISINE
Freshwater fish (salmon, trout, pike, eels), river fowl (ducks, geese), eel
pies, fish stews. Imported spices from downriver trade. Salt from river
estuaries. Ale, some wine from traders.
Lord's Table: Exotic fish dishes, spices from distant lands, fine wine. Shows
trade connections.
Commoner's Table: Fish stew, eel pie, dark bread, small beer. Fresh fish daily.
Festival Foods: The Great Fish Fry (entire village cooks fish together),
riverboat feasts.
Regional Specialties:
Delta eel pie — spiced, wrapped in pastry
Salt-cured salmon — preserves for winter
River mussels — steamed with herbs
E.5 EASTERN FORESTS (TRIBAL) CUISINE
Game (venison, boar, rabbit), berries, nuts, maple syrup, wild herbs, mushrooms.
No grains (traded for). Meat smoked or dried. Little salt — herbs for flavor.

-- 354 of 529 --

Chief's Table: Generous portions of game, maple-sweetened dishes, nuts and
berries. Shows hunting skill and forest knowledge.
Commoner's Table: Smoked meat, dried berries, nuts, herbal teas. What the hunt
provides.
Festival Foods: Whole roasted boar, berry feasts, maple candy for children.
Regional Specialties:
Maple-glazed venison — hunter's pride
Dried berry cakes — travel food
Mushroom and herb soup — gathered fresh
E.6 EASTERN FORESTS (SETTLED) CUISINE
Venison, pork (domesticated pigs in forest clearings), forest mushrooms, dark
beer, honey, apples. Some grains (imported or grown in clearings). Preserved
meats for winter.
Lord's Table: Roasted boar, venison pies, mushroom dishes, dark ale. Shows
mastery of both hunting and farming.
Commoner's Table: Pork stew, dark bread, mushrooms, small beer. Apples in
season.
Festival Foods: Harvest feast with roasted meats, apple tarts, honey mead.
Regional Specialties:
Forest mushroom pie — wild mushrooms in pastry
Honey-roasted pork — lord's favorite
Apple brandy — distilled, strong
E.7 SOUTHERN MOUNTAINS CUISINE
Hardy grains (oats, barley), goat cheese and meat, stone-baked bread, mountain
herbs, game, some pork. Little imported — self-sufficient. Strong ale, some
whiskey.
Lord's Table: Multiple cheeses, fresh bread, roasted goat or mutton, whiskey.
Shows self-sufficiency and herd wealth.
Commoner's Table: Oat porridge, goat cheese, barley bread, ale. Meat when animal
slaughtered.
Festival Foods: Whole roasted goat, cheese wheel rolling (game), whiskey
tasting.
Regional Specialties:
Mountain goat cheese — aged in caves
Stone-baked barley bread — dense, filling
Heather ale — brewed with mountain heather
E.8 NORTHERN SNOWLANDS CUISINE
Salted and preserved fish, salted meats (reindeer, seal), hardy roots (turnips,
carrots), minimal spice, strong ale. Fresh food only in summer. Blood sausages,
dried fish.

-- 355 of 529 --

Lord's Table: Fresh meat (rare, impressive), well-preserved stores, ale that
flows freely. Shows survival skill and preparation.
Commoner's Table: Dried fish, blood sausage, root stew, ale. Fresh food only in
summer.
Festival Foods: Midwinter feast with preserved delicacies, the First Catch
celebration when ice breaks.
Regional Specialties:
Dried reindeer meat — chewy, lasts years
Blood sausage with herbs — hearty
Cloudberry preserve — rare summer treat
E.9 NOMAD STEPPE (TRADITIONAL) CUISINE
Horse meat (honored), fermented mare's milk (airag), blood sausage, dried meat,
cheese, flatbreads traded from settled folk. Little vegetable matter — herds
provide.
Khan's Table: Roasted horse, airag in plenty, dried meat for days. Shows herd
wealth and hospitality.
Commoner's Table: Dried meat, cheese, airag. Meat fresh when animal slaughtered.
Festival Foods: The Great Roast (whole horse), airag drinking contests, dried
meat sharing.
Regional Specialties:
Roasted horse — honored dish
Airag — fermented mare's milk, slightly alcoholic
Dried meat strips — travel food
E.10 NOMAD STEPPE (SETTLED) CUISINE
Blended traditions: horse meat and rice, grilled meats with spices, pilafs,
bread, pastries influenced by settled neighbors. Airag still served, but now in
fine cups.
Khan-King's Table: The best of both worlds — steppe abundance and settled
refinement. Shows mastery of two ways.
Commoner's Table: Rice pilaf with meat, flatbreads, tea. Airag on special
occasions.
Festival Foods: Fusion feasts with both steppe and settled dishes.
Regional Specialties:
Spiced horse meat with rice — steppe-settled fusion
Fried bread with honey — breakfast favorite
Tea with milk and salt — nomad influence
END OF APPENDIX E — REGIONAL CUISINE

-- 443 of 529 --

text
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╗
║ REGIONAL DOCTRINE REFERENCE — SECTION 50
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╣
║
║
║ CENTRAL PLAINS — THE THUNDER CHARGE
║
║ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
║
║ • Preferred: Charge, open ground
║
║ • Avoid: Forests, sieges, rough terrain
║
║ • Composition: 40-60% Heavy Cavalry, 20-30% Heavy Infantry
║
║ • Special: First Charge (may break enemy line round 1)
║
║ • Counter: Rough terrain, feigned retreat
║
║ • Famous: "There is no problem that cannot be solved by riding at it very
fast." ║
║
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╣
║
║
║ WESTERN RIVERS — THE FLOOD TIDE
║
║ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
║
║ • Preferred: Flank, withdraw, water, rivers
║
║ • Avoid: Open plains, no water access
║
║ • Composition: 30-40% Light Infantry, 20-30% River Boats
║
║ • Special: Ebb and Flow (withdraw without pursuit)
║
║ • Counter: Block rivers, force open battle
║
║ • Famous: "Fight, fall back, fight again. The river does not stop flowing."
║
║
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╣
║
║
║ EASTERN FORESTS (SETTLED) — THE HUNTER'S NOOSE
║
║ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
║
║ • Preferred: Ambush, forest, encirclement
║
║ • Avoid: Open plains, cavalry charges
║
║ • Composition: 40-50% Archers, 20-30% Light Infantry
║
║ • Special: The Noose Tightens (encircle after 2 rounds)

-- 444 of 529 --

║
║ • Counter: Burn forest, fight in open
║
║ • Famous: "Let them come into the forest. The trees have teeth."
║
║
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╣
║
║
║ EASTERN FORESTS (TRIBAL) — THE GREEN SHADOWS
║
║ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
║
║ • Preferred: Ambush, vanish, never fair fight
║
║ • Avoid: Open field, prolonged engagement
║
║ • Composition: 60-80% Skirmishers, 20-40% Archers
║
║ • Special: Strike and Vanish (escape without trace)
║
║ • Counter: Bring dogs, clear the forest, stay in formation
║
║ • Famous: "They never saw us. They never saw anything."
║
║
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╣
║
║
║ SOUTHERN MOUNTAINS — THE STONE WALL
║
║ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
║
║ • Preferred: Defend, brace, hold passes
║
║ • Avoid: Attacking in open, pursuing
║
║ • Composition: 40-50% Heavy Infantry, 20-30% Crossbowmen
║
║ • Special: The Unbroken Line (+1 AC per round held)
║
║ • Counter: Starve them out, don't attack the pass
║
║ • Famous: "They can have the valleys. The passes are ours."
║
║
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

║
║ • Preferred: Hold, advance, outlast
║
║ • Avoid: Summer, rivers, fire weapons
║

-- 445 of 529 --

║
║ • Special: The Iron Wall (+1 AC per round held, then +1d6 on advance)
║
║ • Counter: Fire arrows, fight in summer, avoid melee
║

║

║

║

║

G.53 — SCHEMER PLOT GENERATOR (Per Patch 7.26)
text

║ SCHEMER PLOT GENERATOR — SECTION 62
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═╣
║
║
║ Roll 1d6 for plot type:
║
║ ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
║
║
║
║ 1 — LIES:
║
║ "Lord [A] is plotting against Lord [B]. I have proof."
║
║ Effect: -2 Opinion between A and B
║
║ Discovery: DC 12 to trace to Schemer
║
║
║
║ 2 — BLACKMAIL:
║
║ "Lord [C] has a secret. I know what it is. He will serve."
║
║ Effect: One minor lord becomes Schemer's agent
║
║ Discovery: Agent may confess if caught
║
║
║
║ 3 — SABOTAGE:
║
║ "The bridge at [Location] burned last night. No one knows why."

-- 470 of 529 --

║
║ Effect: Trade route disrupted for 1d6 months
║
║ Discovery: Investigators may find traces
║
║
║
║ 4 — FRAME:
║
║ "Lord [D]'s seal was found on the assassin's dagger. He claims innocence."
║
║ Effect: Lord D accused, -3 Opinion all, possible feud
║
║ Discovery: DC 15 to prove forgery
║
║
║
║ 5 — KILL:
║
║ "Lord [E] died in his sleep. Peacefully, they say. But the servants whisper."
║
║ Effect: Assassination attempt (use Part 95)
║
║ Discovery: Assassin may talk
║
║
║
║ 6 — CHAOS:
║
║ "Peasants in [Holding] have risen up. They demand the lord's head."
║
║ Effect: Rebellion in one holding (-2 loyalty)
║
║ Discovery: Investigators may find outside agitators

SECTION 4: WEEKLY REGIONAL UPDATES
Each week during the Weekly World Turn (Part 13.7), system MUST update each
regional ledger with:
4.1 Relationship Shifts
Roll 1d6 for each house pair with active relationship:
1: Deteriorates (-1 Opinion)
2-4: Stable
5-6: Improves (+1 Opinion)
4.2 Event Generation
Check Part 110-115 for rare events in each region.
4.3 Succession Checks
For lords aged 60+: roll 1d6 monthly for death/natural causes.
4.4 Military Changes
Update estimated military strength based on:

-- 513 of 529 --

Recent battles
Recruitment
Losses
SECTION 5: SAVE FILE INTEGRATION

5.2 G.5 Deprecation Note
G.5 (Noble House Ledger) is now DEPRECATED and replaced by the six regional
ledgers. G.5 may be kept for legacy saves but should not be used for new
campaigns.
SECTION 6: LOAD PROTOCOL — REGIONAL RESTORATION
When loading a save, system MUST:
Extract all six G.Rx ledgers
Restore each region's complete house data
Verify cross-region relationships
Reconcile any player-affiliated houses across ledgers
Confirm regional statistics match saved totals
SECTION 7: PLAYER INTERACTIONS
7.1 Viewing Regional Ledgers
Players may request:
"Show me Central Plains houses"
"What's happening in the Northern Snowlands?"
"Show me G.R3"
7.2 Updating Ledgers
System MUST update relevant regional ledger whenever player:

-- 515 of 529 --

Forms alliance with a house
Marries into a house
Defeats a house
Receives tribute from a house
Learns new information about a house
7.3 Cross-Region Tracking
If a house has interests in multiple regions (e.g., marriage alliances), it
appears in all relevant regional ledgers with cross-reference notes.
SECTION 8: REGIONAL RELATIONSHIP MATRIX
For quick reference, system maintains a regional relationship matrix:
text
REGIONAL RELATIONSHIP MATRIX
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
| North | Steppe | West | East | Plains | South
──────────────|───────|────────|──────|──────|────────|──────
Northern | X | -1 | +0 | +0 | -1 | -2
Nomad Steppe | -1 | X | +1 | -1 | +0 | -1
Western Rivers| +0 | +1 | X | +0 | +2 | +0
Eastern Forest| +0 | -1 | +0 | X | +0 | +1
Central Plains| -1 | +0 | +2 | +0 | X | +1
Southern Mtn | -2 | -1 | +0 | +1 | +1 | X
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Values: -3 (War) to +3 (Alliance)
This matrix updates as regional relationships shift through gameplay.

SECTION 3: GENERATION RULES
3.1 Fixed Houses Per Region
Each region starts with 3-4 named Great Houses from G.30 House Name Registry.

-- 517 of 529 --

3.2 Minor Lord Generation
Use Part A.102-A.108 to generate 8-10 minor lords per region:
Territory type (A.103)
Military strength (A.104)
Personality (A.105)
Ambition (A.106)
Loyalty to liege (A.107)
Vassal status (A.108)
3.3 Landed Knight Generation
Generate 11-12 landed knights per region:
Name from regional tables (Part 16.6)
Holding type (Hamlet/Village)
Liege lord (random from Great Houses or Minor Lords)
Personal combat stats (Tier 1-2)
SECTION 4: WORLD LEDGER INTEGRATION
Each regional ledger G.R1-G.R6 MUST track:
All 22-26 noble characters
Relationships between houses
Current lords and heirs
Military estimates
Economic standing

MEGA PATCH 57.0 — OPTIONAL NECROMANCER LORD SYSTEM
text
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╗
║
║
║ MEGA PATCH 57.0
║
║ OPTIONAL NECROMANCER LORD SYSTEM

-- 518 of 529 --

║
║
║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╣
║
║
║ Version: 3.7 — All patches 1.0 through 57.0 integrated
║
║
║
║ THIS SYSTEM IS OPTIONAL.
║
║ Default world: NO MAGIC.
║
║ Magic exists ONLY if player chooses this path.
║
║ Player is the ONLY magic user in the world.
║
║
║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╝
SECTION 1: SYSTEM LAUNCHER ADDITION
Add to System Launcher:
text
[8] NECROMANCER LORD (OPTIONAL)
Warning: This enables magic. You will be the only magic user.
The world has no magic except what you bring.
Select only if you want to be a unique force.
SECTION 2: CORE RULES
2.1 Magic Is Optional
Rule Details
Default world No magic
Magic exists Only if player chooses Necromancer Lord
Other magic users None
NPC necromancers None
Competing undead None
Magical creatures None
2.2 Player Is Unique
You are the only source of magic
No one understands what you are
No one knows how to counter you
No one knows if you can be killed
SECTION 3: CHARACTER CREATION
3.1 New Profession
NECROMANCER LORD — You speak to the dead. They listen. No one else can do what
you do.
3.2 Starting Resources
Resource Amount
Silverdew 50 SD
Food 2 FSU
Soul Essence 20
Living Followers 0
Undead 10 skeletons
Control Used 10/50
3.3 Creation Steps
STEP 1 — NAME & ORIGIN

-- 519 of 529 --

Character name
Age
Physical appearance
Gender
Your story
STEP 2 — STARTING REGION
Northern Snowlands
Nomad Steppe
Western Rivers
Eastern Forests
Central Plains
Southern Mountains
STEP 3 — STARTING RESOURCES
Record all starting resources on character sheet.
SECTION 4: SOUL ESSENCE
4.1 What Is Soul Essence
Soul Essence is spiritual energy from the dead. It is spent to create undead. No
upkeep costs.
4.2 Gaining Soul Essence
Source Essence
Kill living humanoid (you) 1
Corpse found (any) 1
Grave discovered 1d3
Battlefield (per 10 bodies) 1d6
Ancient tomb explored 2d10
Defeat living lord 10
Massacre site (100+ bodies) 50
4.3 Spending Soul Essence
Action Cost
Raise corpse 2
Raise with armor/weapons 2 + (equipment value ÷ 10)
Create undead mount 5
Raise knight/lord corpse 5
Create monstrous undead 10 + component costs
Increase Control Limit 50 per +10
SECTION 5: STANDARD UNDEAD
5.1 Raising Process
Find corpse
Pay Soul Essence cost
1 hour ritual
Undead rises with all equipment intact
Add to Control Used
5.2 Equipment Costs
Equipment Cost Modifier

-- 520 of 529 --

No armor/weapon +0
Leather armor +1
Chain armor +2
Plate armor +3
Simple weapon (dagger, spear) +1
Martial weapon (sword, axe, bow) +2
Heavy weapon (halberd, greatsword) +3
Mount +5
Total Cost = 2 + all modifiers
5.3 Weapon Dice (Part 34)
Weapon Base Dice
Fists 2d6
Dagger 2d6
Sword 3d6
Spear 3d6
Axe 4d6
Halberd 4d6
Mace 3d6
Bow 3d6
Lance 3d6 (+2d6 on Charge)
5.4 Armor Class (Part 34)
Armor AC
None 2
Leather 3
Chain 4
Plate 5
SECTION 6: UNDEAD MOUNTS
6.1 Raising Mounted Undead
Requirements:
Rider corpse
Mount corpse
Pay rider cost + mount cost
2 hour ritual
6.2 Mount Costs
Mount Cost
Riding Horse corpse 5
Warhorse corpse 5
Destrier corpse 5
6.3 Mount Bonuses (Part 36)
Mount Initiative Rerolls Charge Bonus
Riding Horse +1 Reroll 1 missed die +1d6
Warhorse +2 Reroll 2 missed dice +2d6
Destrier +1 Reroll 3 missed dice +3d6
6.4 Mount Death
If mounted unit reduced to half strength:
Roll 1d6 per mount
1-2: Mount killed (unit becomes foot)
3-4: Mount injured (-1 Initiative next round)
5-6: Mount survives
SECTION 7: CONTROL SYSTEM
7.1 Control Limit Formula
text
Control Limit = Necromancer Tier × 50

-- 521 of 529 --

Tier Control Limit
1 50
2 100
3 200
4 400
5 800
7.2 Increasing Control
Essence Spent Control Increase
50 +10
100 +20
250 +50
500 +100
7.3 Exceeding Control
Excess Effect
1-50 All undead -1 to Attack Dice
51-100 As above + 1d10 undead go rogue
101+ All undead turn on you
7.4 Regaining Control
Spend 10 essence per 10 rogue undead
1 hour ritual
Roll 1d6: 4+ success
SECTION 8: UNDEAD GARRISONS
8.1 Garrison Rule
When you control a holding, assign undead as garrison. They:
Do NOT count toward Control Limit
Cannot be moved
Defend automatically
Require NO maintenance
8.2 Garrison Capacity (Part 68)
Holding Max Garrison
Hamlet 10
Village 25
Large Village 50
Town 100
Large Town 200
City 400
Castle 300
Capital 800
8.3 Assigning Garrison
Select undead in holding
Declare as garrison (1 hour)
Remove from Control Used
Add to holding defense
SECTION 9: COMBAT (Part 48)
9.1 Attack Dice Pool Formula
text
Attack Dice = Weapon Dice + (People ÷ 20)d6 + Terrain d6 + Mount Bonus
If led by commander (you or lieutenant), add commander's Tier as d6.
9.2 Damage Resolution
Each d6 vs target AC:

-- 522 of 529 --

Roll < AC = no effect
Roll ≥ AC = 1 kill
Natural 6 = 2 kills
9.3 Undead Special Rules
No morale checks
No wages
No food
No retreat
SECTION 10: REPUTATION (Part 84)

Score Title Effect
0-10 Unknown No one knows you

41-50 Death Lord Armies march
51+ Lich King Adventurers hunt you
10.2 Gaining Reputation
Action Gain

Win battle with undead +2
Capture holding +10
Living witness undead (per 10) +1
Defeat living lord +5
Become Lich +20

SECTION 14: CUSTOM CREATION PROCESS
14.1 Step 1 — Design
Decide what you want to build. Describe it.
Examples:
Knight torso + horse lower body
Soldier + 3 pairs of arms
Humanoid + spider lower body
Multiple torsos + thick hide
14.2 Step 2 — Gather Components
List each component and source.
Example:
1 Knight torso (elite)
1 Horse lower body (common)
2 Human arms (soldier)
14.3 Step 3 — Calculate Cost
Base Cost = 10
Per component:

-- 524 of 529 --

Common: +2
Soldier: +3
Elite: +5
Lord: +8
Complexity:
2-3 components: +0
4-5 components: +5
6-7 components: +10
8+ components: +20
Total = 10 + sum(component costs) + complexity
14.4 Step 4 — Determine Stats
Tier = Average of component tiers (round up)
AC = 3 + highest armor:
Leather: +1
Chain: +2
Plate: +3
Thick hide: +1 (stacks)
Weapon Dice:
Fists: 2d6
Armed arms: weapon dice from Part 34
Claws: 3d6
Multiple arms: +1d6 per extra pair
Initiative:
Human legs: +0
Quadruped: +1
No legs: -2
Movement:
Human legs: Normal
Quadruped: Fast
No legs: Slow
Special (from components):
Claws: Can make claw attack

-- 525 of 529 --

Multiple arms: +1 attack per extra pair
Thick hide: +1 AC
14.5 Step 5 — Name
Every creation must be named.
14.6 Step 6 — Creation Time
Components Time
2-3 2 days
4-5 4 days
6-7 7 days
8+ 14 days
SECTION 15: CONTROL COST FOR MONSTROUS
15.1 Formula
text
Control Cost = (Tier × 3) + (number of components)
Add to Control Used once.
SECTION 16: EXAMPLE CREATIONS
16.1 Knight-Mare
Components: Knight torso (elite), horse body (common), 2 arms (soldier)
Cost: 10 + 5 + 2 + 3+3 + 0 = 23 essence
Stats:
Tier: 3
AC: 5 (plate)
Weapon: Greatsword (4d6)
Initiative: +1
Movement: Fast
Special: Charge (+2d6)
Control: (3×3=9) + 4 = 13
16.2 Six-Fist
Components: Soldier torso, 3 pairs arms (soldier)
Cost: 10 + 3 + 3+3+3 + 5 = 27 essence
Stats:
Tier: 2
AC: 3 (leather)
Weapon: 3 attacks at 3d6 each
Initiative: +0
Movement: Normal
Special: 3 attacks per round
Control: (2×3=6) + 4 = 10
16.3 Wall-Walker
Components: Soldier torso, spider body (common), 2 arms (soldier)

-- 526 of 529 --

Cost: 10 + 3 + 2 + 3+3 + 0 = 21 essence
Stats:
Tier: 2
AC: 3
Weapon: Claws (3d6) + arms (3d6)
Initiative: +0
Movement: Normal, climb any surface
Special: +2 to ambush
Control: (2×3=6) + 3 = 9

• Hold 3+ holdings with garrisons
• Create 3+ monstrous undead
• Become Lich
• Achieve reputation 50+
Reward: Immortality
+200 Control Limit
You are the only magic user in history
